<keywords>The Black Pullet, grimoires, Dr. Encausse, Papus, talismans, Goetic magic, Cornelius Agrippa, occult forgery, eighteenth-century magic</keywords>

--- Page 94 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of "The Black Pullet," focusing on its various editions, its grandly exaggerated title, and the fictional narrative that serves as its origin story.</meta>

*The Black Pullet* was reprinted several times during its own era with minor changes—once under the title *The Treasure of the Old Man of the Pyramids*, which was followed by a sequel or companion work called the *Black Screech Owl*. It was reprinted in recent years in Paris in an edition intended for book collectors <note>original: bibliophiles</note>, though it shows no evidence of actual scholarly research. Despite the modest claims mentioned previously, the title of the original edition is quite extravagant: "The Black Pullet, or the Hen with the Golden Eggs, comprising the Science of Magical Talismans and Rings, the Art of Necromancy and of the Kabalah <note>An esoteric method of interpreting Hebrew scripture</note>, for the Conjuration of Aerial and Infernal Spirits, of Sylphs, Undines, and Gnomes; useful for acquiring the Secret Sciences, for the Discovery of Treasures, for obtaining power to command all beings, and to unmask all Sciences and Bewitchments. The whole following the Doctrines of Socrates, Pythagoras, Zoroaster (Son of the Great Aromasis), and other philosophers whose manuscript works escaped the burning of the Library of Ptolemy. Translated from the Language of the Magi and the Language of the Hieroglyphs by the Doctors Mizzaboula-Jabamîa, Danhuzerus, Nehmahmiah, Judahim, and Eliaeb. Rendered into French by A. J. S. D. R. L. G. F." The place of publication is listed as Egypt (which likely means Rome), and the date is given as 740 (meaning 1740); however, as we shall see, this date is false. It can be said immediately that the text makes no real attempt to fulfill the magnificent promises made in its title.

The preface begs that the *Black Pullet* not be confused with the collections of delusions and errors which

<margin>p. 115</margin>

so many authors have tried to make credible by claiming they produce supernatural effects. After considering the book, most readers will find it impossible to grant this request. The work, as previously stated, is a work of fiction <note>original: romance</note>, and the first thing it clarifies is that even adding a thousand years to the date on the title page is not enough to make it accurate. <note>The author argues that the book was written much later than 1740, likely in the early 19th century.</note> It is the story of a man who "was part of the expedition to Egypt" and was "an officer in the army of the genius" <note>original: "l'armée du génie"—this likely refers to the French Engineering Corps during Napoleon’s Egyptian Campaign (1798–1801)</note>. This is clearly a reference to Napoleon, and at best, the date of composition is only a little more than a century ago. 

According to the story, while in Egypt, the narrator was sent on an expedition to the Pyramids accompanied by some light cavalry <note>original: chasseurs</note>. They were eating lunch under the shadow of the "grand colossus" <note>likely the Great Sphinx</note> when they were attacked by a horde of desert Arabs. The narrator's comrades were killed, and he was left for dead on the ground. When he regained consciousness, he gave himself up to mournful thoughts, expecting to die. He was delivering a farewell speech to the setting sun when a stone in the Pyramid rolled back and a venerable old man stepped out, who appeared to be a Turk because of his turban. This man found the bodies scattered across the desert and identified their nationality. When he examined the officer and saw he was still alive (the officer kissed the old man's hand to show he was breathing), the old man took pity on him. Setting aside any prejudice that a typical Muslim <note>original: Mussulman</note> might feel toward an enemy based on patriotism or religion, he revived the narrator with a wonderful medicinal drink <note>original: cordial</note> that immediately healed the wounded man. The officer then followed his rescuer into the Pyramid, which turned out to be the old man's home and a massive temple of Magic. Inside were vast halls, endless galleries, and underground chambers filled with treasures.

<summary>This page describes the extravagant claims and fictional origin story of "The Black Pullet," a grimoire that pretends to be an ancient mystical text but is actually a 19th-century French occult romance set during Napoleon's Egyptian campaign.</summary>
<keywords>The Black Pullet, Grimoires, Napoleon, Egyptology, Magical Talismans, Occult Fiction, Necromancy, Kabalah</keywords>

--- Page 95 ---
<meta>This page continues the summary of the fictional narrative within "The Black Pullet," describing the training of the French officer by the mysterious sage and the officer's eventual return to Provence.</meta>

<page-num>116</page-num>

apparitions of blazing lamps, innumerable ministering spirits, and magic suppers; above all else, there was the Black Pullet. In short, daily <note>original: "diurnal"</note> life was characterized throughout by the supernatural; it was an organized <note>original: "methodised"</note> version of Aladdin with an inner meaning provided by Astaroth <note>A high-ranking demon in the grimoire tradition, often depicted as a crowned man riding a dragon-like beast</note>. The sage himself proved to be the sole heir of the Magi <note>Ancient Persian priest-magicians</note> and the creators of those Egyptian hieroglyphics which are the "despair of the learned." Furthermore, he was himself searching for an heir, as he felt his own death was approaching. In short, the French officer acquired the Turkish language by using a grammar book rooted in sorcery. This enabled him to converse with his protector—which, on the whole, seems unnecessary, considering his protector possessed a talisman that granted immediate fluency in all languages. The officer was then instructed in the powers and wonders of twenty-two talismanic symbols and their corresponding rings, as well as the secret of creating the Black Pullet, which was more skilled at finding gold than a divining rod is at discovering water. After these instructions, despite many prayers and the services of the spirit <note>original: "genius"</note> *Odous*, the righteous man died upon a sofa, while his fortunate philosophical successor <note>original: "kinsman in philosophy"</note> fainted at his benefactor's feet. In due time, accompanied by the spirit who had been transferred to his service, the French officer managed to depart from Egypt, laden with treasures and carrying the ashes of the sage in a costly urn. He took a ship for Marseilles, calmed a storm during the voyage, and returned to his native country. He made his home in Provence, spending his days in experiments with the Black Pullet, or in study, meditation, and walking. He eventually undertook the task of writing this memorial of his good fortune, in which he threatens the publishers of any pirated edition that he will use a talisman to adorn them with ears six inches longer than those of 

<page-num>117</page-num>

Midas <note>In Greek mythology, King Midas was punished by the god Apollo with the ears of a donkey</note>. But this does not seem to have stopped the publishers.

*The Black Pullet* denies any connection with Black Magic, yet it is properly identified with it—firstly by its symbols <note>original: "characters"</note> and secondly by their claimed power over evil spirits. However, it should be noted that the hellish beings mentioned in the title are not, on the surface, devils, but Salamanders—that is to say, elemental spirits of fire. But while it thus goes beyond Black Magic—if it is necessary to make such a distinction—it is not above plagiarism; consequently, it incorporates many pages from the *Comte de Gabalis* <note>A famous 1670 satirical work by Abbé de Villars that discussed elemental spirits</note>.

For the summoning <note>original: "evocation"</note> of the spirits <note>original: "genii"</note> who served the Old Man of the Pyramid, it is enough to say: THOMATOS, BENESSER, FLIANTER. You are then likely to be surrounded by thirty-three distinct intelligences. To gain their respect, say: LITAN, IZER, OSNAS, and they will bow down before you, each individually saying: NANTHER. The words SOUTRAM, UBARSINENS will cause them to carry you through the air to wherever you wish to go. Upon speaking the single word RABIAM, they will return you to your own home. It is necessary, however, to be protected by the talismans and rings of the master, but these can be obtained through an inexpensive process. In the workspace <note>original: "cabinet"</note> of the Old Man of the Pyramid, they were made of precious metals and glittered with gems, but they are believed to serve all practical purposes even if the rings are made of

<summary>This page concludes the fictional origin story of "The Black Pullet" and begins analyzing its magical contents, noting its dependence on earlier works like the Comte de Gabalis and listing specific incantations for summoning spirits.</summary>

## § 7. Talismans of the Sage of the Pyramids

I. This talisman is used for the conjuration of heavenly and hellish <note>original: "celestial and infernal"</note> powers. It should be embroidered in silver on sky-blue satin. The summoning <note>original: "evoking"</note> words are **SIRAS, ETAR, BESANAR**, at which point many spirits will appear.

II. Grants the love and devotion <note>original: "complaisance"</note> of all women. It should be embroidered in silver on black satin. The summoning words are **NADES, SURADIS, MANINER**, pronounced while the ring—which should be on the middle finger of the left hand—is pressed against the lips. This ritual <note>original: "conjuration"</note> ensures the appearance of a spirit <note>original: "genius"</note> with rose-colored wings. If addressed with the words **SADER, PROSTAS, SOLASTER**, this spirit will travel through all space to bring you the woman you love, even if she were the queen of a Caliph's harem <note>original: "seraglio"</note>. At the words **MAMMES, LAHER**, she will be taken away by four slaves. It will be observed that this is a form of vulgar magical humor <note>original: "occult ribaldry"</note>.

**III.** This talisman discovers all hidden treasures and ensures their possession. The design of the talisman should be embroidered in gold upon green satin. Pronouncing the words **ONAÏM, PERANTÈS, RASONASTOS** will cause the appearance of seven spirits <note>original: "genii"</note> with dark complexions <note>original: "au teint bazané"</note>. Each of these spirits will pour out golden coins <note>original: "ducats"</note> from great leather bags at the feet of the sorcerer; the operations of this grimoire <term>a textbook of magic</term> are performed on a massive scale. Furthermore <note>original: "Item"</note>, a black hooded bird will be perched upon the shoulder of each spirit.

**IV.** This talisman discovers the most hidden secrets and enables its possessor to penetrate everywhere unseen. The talisman should be made of violet satin, with the figures embroidered in silver. It must be held in the left hand, on which the ring should also be worn. It should be placed close to the ear while pronouncing the words **NITRÆ, RADOU, SUNANDAM**, at which point a distinct voice will speak the desired mystery.

**V.** This talisman will make the most reserved <note>original: "taciturn"</note> man reveal <note>original: "unbosom"</note> his heart to its owner, whose enemies will also be forced to confess all their schemes <note>original: "machinations"</note>. The addition of the word **BRANTHER** will force the enemies of the owner to declare their projects aloud.

**VI.** This talisman sets to work enough spirits <note>original: "genii"</note> for the immediate achievement of any work which the owner may desire to undertake, and for the stopping of any work which may oppose him. The talisman should be made of lilac satin with the figures embroidered in shaded silk. The magical words are **ZORAMI, ZAITUX, ELASTOT**.

**VII.** This talisman has the power to destroy everything; it can cause hail, thunderbolts, and stars to fall from heaven <note>The "falling stars" likely refer to meteors or a symbolic disruption of the celestial order.</note>, as well as trigger earthquakes, storms, and similar disasters. Simultaneously, it protects the owner's friends from any accidents. The design <note>original: "figure"</note> of the talisman should be embroidered in silver thread upon poppy-red satin <note>The poppy was a traditional symbol for sleep and death.</note>. The magical words are: 

1. **DITAU, HURANDOS**, for acts of destruction; 
2. **RIDAS, TALIMOL**, to command the elements; 
3. **ATROSIS, NARPIDA**, for the fall of hail, etc.; 
4. **UUSUR, ITAR**, to cause earthquakes; 
5. **HISPEN, TROMADOR**, for hurricanes and storms; 
6. **PARANTHES, HISTANOS**, for the protection <note>original: "preservation"</note> of friends.

**VIII.** This talisman grants invisibility, even to the eyes of spirits <note>original: "genii" — in this context, supernatural beings or elemental guardians often found in Middle Eastern and occult lore</note>, so that God alone shall witness the actions of the possessor. It is accompanied by the power of penetrating any location and passing through brick walls. The magical words are **BENATIR, CARARKAU, DEDOS, ETINARMI**. For each operation, the ring must be placed upon a different finger of the right hand. The talisman is made of yellow satin embroidered with black silk.

**IX.** This talisman transports the possessor to any part of the world without danger. The potent words are **RADITUS, POLASTRIEN, TERPANDU, OSTRATA, PERICATUR, ERMAS**. The talisman is made of puce-colored <note>A dark reddish-purple or brownish-purple color</note> satin embroidered with gold.

**X.** This talisman opens all locks at a touch, regardless of whatever precautions have been taken to secure them. The magical words are **SARITAP, PERNISOX, OTTARIM**. The talisman is made of deep blue satin embroidered with silver.

**XI.** This talisman places the possessor in any desired house without the need for entering through a door and allows them to read the thoughts of all people, so that they can be helped or harmed at will. The talisman is made of light grey satin embroidered with gold. To know someone's thoughts, place the talisman on your head, breathe upon the ring, and say: **O TAROT <note>In the context of this grimoire, "Tarot" is used as a word of power, though it likely refers to the mystical system of the same name</note>, NIZAEL, ESTARNAS, TANTAREZ.** To help those who are worthy, say: **NISTA, SAPER, VISNOS**, and they will immediately enjoy every kind of prosperity. To punish your enemies or evil persons, say: **XATROS, NIFER, ROXAS, TORTOS**, and they will be immediately delivered to frightful torments.

**XII.** This talisman destroys all plots formed against the possessor and compels rebellious spirits <note>original: "rebellious spirits" — in ritual magic, these are entities that resist the magician's commands</note> to obey. The talisman is made of rose-colored satin embroidered with silver. It should be placed upon a table with the left hand laid upon it; the ring should be worn on the middle finger of the right hand, and the operator, with a bowed head, should repeat in a low voice the words: **SENAPOS, TERFITA, ESTAMOS, PERFITER, NOTARIN.**

**XIII.** This talisman endows the owner with every virtue and talent, as well as the desire to do good. All substances of a harmful or evil quality can be turned into excellent ones by means of it. To gain the first benefit, it is enough to hold up the talisman while wearing the ring on the first joint of the ring finger <note>original: "third finger"</note> of the right hand and pronounce the words: **TURAN, ESTONOS, FUZA**. For the second benefit, say: **VAZOTAS, TESTANAR**. The talisman should be made of saffron-colored satin embroidered with silver.

**XIV.** This talisman provides the knowledge of all minerals and vegetables <note>original: "vegetables"; in this context, it refers to all plant life or botanical substances</note>, along with their virtues and properties. It also grants the "universal medicine" <note>the *panacea* or *elixir of life* sought by alchemists to cure all ills</note> and the ability to heal all sick persons. The talisman is made of orange-colored satin embroidered with silver. It should be worn upon the chest, and the ring should be kept in a small pouch <note>original: "locket (kerchief)"; likely a small fabric bag or wrap</note> around the neck, secured by a ribbon of flame-colored silk. The words of power <note>original: "operative words"</note> are: **RETERREM, SALIBAT, CRATARES, HISATER**.

**XV.** This talisman provides protection from the most ferocious animals and the means of overcoming them. It also grants the knowledge of animal language and drives away rabid <note>original: "mad"</note> animals. The talisman should be made of deep green satin embroidered with gold. For the first three purposes, say: **HOCATOS, IMORAD, SURATER, MARKILA**. To drive away rabid animals, say: **TRUMANTREM, RICONA, ESTUPIT, OXA**.

**XVIII.** Grants good fortune in any lottery. The talisman is made of cherry-red <note>original: "cerise-coloured"</note> satin embroidered with gold and silver. It should be bound to the left arm with a white ribbon, and the ring must be worn on the little finger of the right hand. The magical words are: **ROKES** for a winning number, **PILATUS** for a double-ace <term>ambes-ace</term>, **ZOTOAS** for a group of ten <term>denary</term>, **TULITAS** for a group of four <term>quaternary</term>,

**XATANITOS** for a group of five <term>quinary</term>. One must be careful to pronounce all the words at the "quine" <term>quine</term>—an instruction which I do not quite understand, but if the art or science of the lottery is still practiced today under these old laws, I leave the question to those who are experts in it. This is one of the lesser mysteries of occultism. In card games, the same powerful formula should be repeated while shuffling for yourself or your partner. Before beginning, touch your left arm with your right hand near the talisman, and kiss the ring. These small maneuvers can be carried out, says the "honest" grimoire <note>original: "Grimoire"</note>, without attracting the notice of your opponent.

->## THE GOETIC <note>original: "Goëtic"; referring to black magic or sorcery</note> CIRCLE OF THE BLACK PULLET.<-

Because the range of human ambition recognized by ceremonial magic is always somewhat limited, it is not surprising that the functions of these talismans often overlap. Some of them correspond very closely to the powers attributed to a Mage <note>original: "Magus"</note> by the spiritual science of the *Arbatel* <note>The *Arbatel de Magia Veterum* is a 16th-century grimoire known for its relatively positive, spiritual tone compared to later black magic texts</note>. In the original text, the talismans are preceded by an illustration of a magical rod. According to the accompanying description, this rod is stained with the blood of a lamb and shaped like a serpent. While the description is somewhat unclear, the wand must apparently be flexible so that it can be bent when necessary, allowing the mouth and tail to be joined by a golden chain to form a circle. 

It will be observed that this is the Ouroboros <note>The ancient symbol of a serpent eating its own tail, representing infinity, the cycle of nature, or the unity of all things</note>—the great and mystical symbol—but it has certainly fallen into strange company here! The wand should be six feet long and, when bent, can be used as a circle for summoning spirits <note>original: "evocation"</note>. There is also an illustration of the "grand magical circle," though it is not described in the text. In total, there are twenty-two figures, and the inclusion of the word "TAROT" in the list of summoning terms is significant in this context. A certain connection between these talismans and the Tarot Trumps <note>The 22 Major Arcana cards of the Tarot deck</note> is unmistakable, at least in some instances. This seems to indicate that the work has a more specific occult purpose than it might first appear. It is essentially the symbolism of the Major Arcana redirected toward the powers of the underworld <note>original: "Powers of the Deep"</note>.

### § 8. The Gold-Finding Hen

<summary>This page describes the flexible, serpent-shaped magical rod and the ritual circle used in "The Black Pullet," noting the grimoire's surprising symbolic links to the twenty-two Major Arcana of the Tarot.</summary>
<keywords>The Black Pullet, Arbatel, Magic Rod, Ouroboros, Goetic Circle, Tarot, Major Arcana, ceremonial magic, evocation</keywords>

--- Page 109 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the physical tools of "The Black Pullet" to the core ritual of the grimoire: the creation of the legendary "Gold-Finding Hen." It describes two methods—one aristocratic and one for "persons of small means"—reflecting the author's critical view of these magical claims.</meta>

I must now move on to the hatching <note>original: "incubation"</note> of the gold-finding hen, which is the most important part <note>original: "head and crown"</note> of these procedures. The great Oromasis <note>A name often associated in occult literature with Ahura Mazda, the supreme deity of Zoroastrianism</note>, the father of Zoroaster, was the first person to possess one of these marvelous birds, which are hatched from an egg in the following way. Take aromatic woods—such as aloes, cedar, orange, citron, laurel, orris-root <note>original: "iris-root"</note>, and rose leaves dried in the sun. Place them in a golden burner <note>original: "chafing-dish"</note>; pour balsamic oil over them; and add the finest incense and clear resin <note>original: "gum"</note>. Next, say: **ATHAS, SOLINAM, ERMINATOS, PASAM**; place a glass cover over the burner; direct the sun's rays onto it, and the wood will catch fire and the glass will melt—clearly by the magical power inherent in the operation—filling the area with a sweet scent as the mixture quickly burns to ashes. While they are still red-hot, place these ashes into a golden egg; then lay the

<page-num>131</page-num>

egg upon a black cushion. Cover it with a bell-glass made of faceted rock crystal; then lift up your eyes and stretch your arms toward heaven and cry: **O SANATAPER, ISMAÏ, NONTAPILUS, ERTIVALER, CANOPISTUS**. Expose the glass to the strongest rays of the sun until it seems to be enveloped in flame, until the egg is no longer visible, and until a slight vapor rises. Soon you will see a black young hen <note>original: "pullet"</note> just beginning to move; if you then say **BINUSAS, TESTIPAS**, it will sprout wings and nestle in your chest. It is not mentioned that anything disastrous follows this; rather, a certain advantage is gained, which will be explained in the next paragraph.

While this is the "true" process of the Pyramids—and no doubt easy for a direct heir of Oromasis—it is obvious that it presents difficulties for those born merely under the ordinary moon of sorcery. Being merciful to human limitations, like all its counterparts, this Grimoire provides an alternative method suitable for persons of small means. Take an unspotted egg and expose it to the noon rays of the sun; then select the blackest hen you can find. If it has any colored feathers, pluck them. Provided that the Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Animals <note>Waite is injecting a bit of modern humor here, as the SPCA was well-established by the time he wrote this in 1913</note> does not intervene, manage to hood this bird with a kind of black bonnet drawn over its eyes so that it cannot see. Leave its beak free. Shut it in a box, also lined with black material, and large enough to hold the bird comfortably. Place the box in a room where no daylight can enter; feed the bird only at night; ensure that no noise disturbs it; and set it to hatch the egg. Because everything will be black to the bird, its imagination—which is likely enough—will be overwhelmed by the sense of blackness. If the bird survives this imprisonment, it will ultimately hatch a perfectly black chicken—provided that the practitioner, through his wisdom and virtue, is worthy of participating in such sacred and divine mysteries. It is not explained how or

<page-num>132</page-num>

why a hen hatched in such an uncomfortable fashion should have a specific instinct for detecting places where gold is hidden, but that is the faculty attributed to it. One can only conclude that the *Black Pullet* is slightly more foolish than the rest of its idle companions among the Grimoires.

<summary>This passage details the ritual for creating a "gold-finding hen" through either an expensive alchemical process involving a golden egg or a cheaper "budget" method involving sensory deprivation of a live hen. The author, Waite, maintains a skeptical and ironic tone regarding the absurdity of these instructions.</summary>

<keywords>The Black Pullet, Oromasis, Zoroaster, gold-finding hen, alchemy, grimoire, ritual magic, magical incubation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of magical rituals involving black hens, moving from the "Black Pullet" traditions to a more sinister "Goetic" ritual found in the "Red Dragon" grimoire. It describes the specific method for summoning a demon at a crossroads.</meta>

<page-num>133</page-num>

However, from the perspective of sorcery, this simplified process did not seem to be the final word on the subject. Despite the threatened vengeance of the heir of Oromasis <note>A name associated with Zoroastrianism, often used in occult lore to give an air of ancient authority</note>, the mystery was adapted by the later editors of the *Red Dragon* <note>A famous grimoire of black magic, also known as *Le Dragon Rouge*</note> and reappears as the *Grand Kabbalah* <note>original: "Grand Cabala"</note>, without which no other magic can succeed. In this, its final transformation, it becomes a recipe for summoning the devil that seems to embody the true spirit of Goetic <note>original: "Goëtic"; referring to black magic or the invocation of demons</note> genius. 

I must first state that its success depends on the practitioner possessing a focused and devotional spirit, as well as a clean conscience; otherwise, instead of commanding the evil spirit, the spirit will command them. Once this is understood, the process itself is simple. You simply need to obtain a black hen that has never mated <note>original: "never been crossed by the male bird"</note> and do so in a way that prevents it from cackling. It is best, therefore, to catch it while it is asleep, clutching it by the neck—but only tightly enough to keep it from crying out. 

You must go to the main road, walk until you reach a crossroads, and there, at the stroke of midnight, draw a circle with a cypress rod. Stand in the center of the circle and tear the bird in half <note>original: "in twain"</note>, reciting three times the words: *Euphas, Metahîm, frugativi et appellavi* <note>original: pseudo-Latin; "frugativi" is likely a corruption of "fugativi" (put to flight) or "fruitus" (enjoyed), while "appellavi" means "I have called/summoned"</note>. Next, turn to the East, kneel down, recite a prayer, and conclude it with the Grand Invocation <note>original: "Appellation"</note>. At that moment, the Unclean Spirit will appear to you wearing a scarlet outer coat <note>original: "surcoat"</note>, a yellow vest, and pale green breeches. His head will look like a dog's, but his ears will be those of a donkey <note>original: "ass"</note>, with two horns above them; he will also have the legs and hoofs of a calf. He will ask for your orders, which you may give as you please. Since he has no choice but to obey, you may become rich instantly and thus the happiest of men. At least, that is the claim <note>original: "judgment"</note> of the Grimoire. Whether the victim of this process is meant to be torn to pieces while alive is not explicitly...

The spirit will appear, though this can be guessed from the safety measures taken at the start. Because of this, I have adjusted the procedure so that if some foolish person tries to perform it in these more civilized days, they will gain nothing but the effort they spent—at least according to the theories of the ceremonial art <term>ceremonial art: the formal practice of ritual magic, governed by specific rules and correspondences</term>. This is, therefore, a warning to the foolish.

# CHAPTER I

## The Preparation of the Operator

### § 1. Concerning the Love of God

The rites of so-called Transcendental Magic are, by hypothesis <note>original: *ex hypothesi*</note>, divine and religious rites. Even in their meager and childish way, the spiritual and moral advice found in their instructions are vulgarized versions of the <term>counsels of perfection</term> <note>In Christian theology, these are suggestions for those seeking a higher level of spiritual life beyond the basic requirements of the law.</note>, regardless of any absolute lack of logic that might exist in their experiments. 1 The "Composite Rituals" (texts that mix various traditions), despite what an analysis of them reveals, also largely take on the nature of substitute religious observances—sometimes Jewish, sometimes Christian. In both cases, this fact is easily understood. To communicate with the legendary Spirits of the Firmament and to practice an art that offers its masters the spiritual transformation of Enoch—the King of the Inferior World, 2 "who was no more, because God took him"—may well demand a high degree of holiness from the candidate, regardless of where that path ultimately leads him. Furthermore, to dominate the so-called Spirits of the Elements, the Kabbalistic <term>Kliphoth</term> <note>In Jewish mysticism, these are "shells" or "husks" representing evil or impure spiritual forces.</note>, and the Evil Demons, it is reasonable to assume that the

<page-num>140</page-num>

<term>Magus</term> <note>A master of magic or a practitioner of the high occult arts.</note> must be free from common weaknesses and common vices, 1 and must be strengthened by the grace and favor of the higher spiritual world. Given the magical perspective in each case, the conditions required for the operation seem beyond challenge in this regard. Its recommendations, however, were more specifically about timing and seasons; they were for the immediate magical work at hand rather than for the high and transcendent art of living a holy life. 

But it will certainly appear at first glance to be a strange contradiction that Black Magic should also involve religious observances and should demand similar conditions, both internal and external, from those who would undertake its projects. It is not, as generally supposed, either Christianity in reverse <note>original: *à rebours*</note> or a reversed religion of Israel. It is not the intentional profanation of religious ritual and observance; it is something less outrageous but logically more senseless. Insofar as it uses such rituals and observances, it is not to insult God in the interests of demon-worship, but to draw power and virtue from above for the more successful control of Evil Spirits. This holds true whether the operator's purpose is otherwise lawful or not.

The "Divine Love," says the book of *True Black Magic*, 2 must come before the acquisition of the "Science of Solomon," the son of King David, who said: "The beginning and Key of my Wisdom are the fear of God, to do Him honor, to adore Him with great heartbreak and regret for sins, and to call upon His aid in all our intentions and aspirations; by fulfilling this, God will lead us onto the right path." 3

To explain this contradiction, it is not enough to say that

<summary>This page discusses how both "High" and "Black" magic paradoxically require the practitioner to follow religious observances and maintain a state of spiritual purity. Waite explains that even in Black Magic, the goal of religious devotion is to gain divine power to control demonic forces rather than to insult the divine.</summary>
<keywords>Transcendental Magic, Black Magic, King Solomon, Enoch, Kliphoth, religious observance, Magus, spiritual preparation</keywords>

--- Page 115 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the preparation of the magical practitioner, focusing on the paradoxical requirement of religious devotion in even the most sinister magical texts.</meta>

<page-num>141</page-num>

The book of *True Black Magic* is simply the *Key of Solomon* <note>original: *Clavicula Salomonis*; one of the most famous and influential manuals of ceremonial magic, attributed to King Solomon.</note> adapted to <term>Goëtic</term> <note>Referring to Goëtia, a type of magic involving the invocation of demons or spirits, often distinguished from "higher" or "divine" magic.</note> intentions. In the first place, as already seen, it is impossible to find a Goëtic intention in either of the versions of the *Key of Solomon* <note>original: *Clavicles*</note>; and, in the second place, the same religious characteristics are found in the <term>Grimoires</term> <note>Books of magic spells and invocations.</note> which are least derived from the *Key of Solomon*, namely, the *Grimoire of Honorius* and the one called the *Grand Grimoire*. To meditate continually on the task at hand and to center every hope in the infinite goodness of the Great Adonai <note>A Hebrew name for God, meaning "Lord."</note> is the rule established by the latter as the first principle of success. 1

The senseless nature of the processes of Black Magic does not offer an explanation by itself. The attempt to win the favor of God by means of prayers, sacrifices, and <term>abstinence</term> <note>Refraining from food, drink, or physical pleasures as a form of spiritual discipline.</note>, and thus to obtain Divine assistance for the successful completion of hideous crimes and preposterous or impossible tasks, is, of course, madness. The God acknowledged and invoked by Goëtic Magic is not the "Principle of Evil," as the myth of Modern Satanism <note>Waite refers here to late 19th-century French occultist theories about organized devil-worship.</note> supposes, 2 but the "terrible and venerable Deity" Who destroyed the power of the rebellious angels—alternatively the Jehovah of the Jewish rituals and the Trinity of the Christian magical tradition. This insane observance actually followed from the interpretation placed by Goëtic <term>Theurgy</term> <note>Divine work; high magic intended to invoke gods or spirits to achieve spiritual elevation.</note> on the fundamental doctrine of Practical Magic: namely, the power of Divine names to compel the obedience of all spirits to those who could pronounce them. 3

<page-num>142</page-num>

Collections of these words and names were recited as forms of invocation and binding; incorporated into a suitable setting of official prayers, they were used in all magical ceremonies. Black Magic was sometimes the application of these names to more unlawful purposes, and sometimes to the same purposes as high magic. The utterance of the Divine Name—which was supposed to make the devils tremble and place them at the will of the <term>Magus</term> <note>A master of magic.</note>—was at least equally powerful, it was argued, to enforce their obedience for a purpose in harmony with their own nature. Behind this there lay also the unspoken assumption that it was easier to control demons than to persuade angels. Then, seeing that prayer to God and the invocation of the Divine Names presuppose a proper spirit of reverence, devotion, and love as the condition upon which prayer is heard, it became a condition even in Goëtia. The first impossibility required of the expert in Black Magic is, therefore, that he should love God before he bewitches his neighbor; that he should put all his hopes in God before he makes a pact with Satan; that, in a word, he should be good in order to do evil.

### § 2. Concerning Fortitude

Once the spiritual intention of the practitioner was decided, his next step was to acquire the mental attitude appropriate for his future work. We may picture him in the traditional state of the sorcerer—poor, outlawed, envious, ambitious, and possessing no talent for honest work. Unable to earn money, he longs for hidden treasures and frequents those spots throughout the countryside that are rumored to hide them. He has likely done this for

<margin>p. 143</margin>

a long time before deciding to turn to Magic, but the earth will not give up her riches. This is because the <term>gnomes</term> <note>Earth-dwelling spirits in folklore and alchemy.</note> and the Earth-Spirits, the <term>Alastors</term> <note>In Greek mythology, an avenging spirit; in demonology, a cruel or possessing demon.</note>, and the Demons of the Solitudes stand guard over the secrets of dead misers long after the human ghost has stopped walking the neighborhood. He does not hesitate for long when he learns that the <term>Grimoires</term> <note>Manuals of magic, especially those used to summon spirits.</note> of Black Magic are full of dark rituals and deadly, mysterious words that can force those guardians to stay or flee. The Church and the State may threaten him with fire for his flesh and fire for his soul <note>Referring to being burned at the stake and eternal damnation.</note>, but through watchfulness and secrecy, he hopes to escape the—

<summary>This page concludes the philosophical and linguistic foundations of magic before describing the sorcerer as a desperate, marginalized figure driven by poverty and envy to seek forbidden treasure through dark rituals.</summary>
<keywords>regeneration, elementary spirits, Éliphas Lévi, Cornelius Agrippa, words of power, fortitude, sorcerer social status, gnomes, Alastors, black magic motives</keywords>

--- Page 117 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the mental state required for magic and introduces the specific physical requirements of continence and abstinence as prescribed in various grimoires.</meta>

one, and the other is a distant danger. Obviously, however, in order to reach his goal, he must arm himself with courage and caution; this is the first advice given by his guides to the *Holy Kingdom* <note>original: *Sanctum Regnum*</note> of *Goetia* <term>Goetia</term><note>A branch of magic dealing with the summoning of demons or spirits, often contrasted with Theurgy or "high" magic.</note>.

> "O men! O powerless mortals!" cries the author of the *Grand Grimoire* <gloss>1</gloss> <note>The *Grand Grimoire* is one of the most famous black magic manuals, often associated with the summoning of Lucifugé Rofocale.</note>, "tremble at your own recklessness when you blindly aim to possess a science so deep. Lift your minds beyond your limited world and learn from me: before you attempt anything, it is necessary to become firm and unshakeable. Furthermore, you must be scrupulously attentive in following, step by step, every single thing I tell you. Without these precautions, every operation will lead to your disadvantage, confusion, and total destruction. On the other hand, by following my commands exactly, you will rise from your lowly state and poverty, achieving complete success in all your projects. Therefore, arm yourselves with courage, caution, wisdom, and virtue as the necessary traits for this grand and unlimited work, on which I have spent sixty-seven years, working night and day to succeed in such a sublime goal." <gloss>2</gloss>

One would think there were shorter paths to wealth and the satisfaction of other common desires.

## § 3. Concerning Continence and Abstinence

The *Fourth Book of Occult Philosophy*—attributed to Cornelius Agrippa but, as we have seen, actually a collection of various writings falsely credited to him—gives an extremely curious explanation for the rule of sanctity required by all forms of Ceremonial Magic. The tools used in the Art are useless without consecration. The act of consecration is what gives them their power <note>original: "virtue"</note>, and this power comes from two sources: the power of sanctification found in Divine Names and the prayers that use them, and the power of sanctification found in the holiness of the person performing the consecration. <gloss>1</gloss> A necessary part of this holiness was maintaining chastity for a specific—though limited—period before the operation. This was also to be combined with a fast that increased in strictness. In their instructions on these points, the rituals of Black Magic differ very little from their models: the earlier *Keys of Solomon*, the work of pseudo-Agrippa <gloss>2</gloss>, or *The Magical Elements* of Peter de Abano. Here is the instruction for the Neophyte <term>neophyte</term><note>A beginner or a new initiate into a religious or magical system.</note> found in the *Grand Grimoire*:

<summary>This page quotes the Grand Grimoire’s warnings to aspiring sorcerers and explains that the effectiveness of magical tools depends on the practitioner's personal "holiness," achieved through fasting and celibacy.</summary>
<keywords>Grand Grimoire, Goetia, consecration, continence, abstinence, pseudo-Solomon, Cornelius Agrippa, ritual purity, black magic</keywords>

--- Page 118 ---
<meta>This page details the specific preparatory fasts and prayers required by various grimoires, including the Grand Grimoire, the Grimoire of Honorius, and the Grimorium Verum.</meta>

The *Grand Grimoire* states: "You must abstain during an entire lunar quarter from the company of women, so as to protect yourself from the possibility of impurity. You must begin your magical quarter at the same moment as the lunar phase itself, by making a pledge to the Great Adonai <note>original: *Adonay*</note>, who is the Master of all Spirits, to take no more than two meals <term>collations</term><note>A light meal or snack permitted during periods of ritual fasting.</note> daily—that is to say, in every twenty-four hours of the said lunar quarter. These meals should be taken at noon and midnight, or, if it pleases you better, at seven o'clock in the morning and at the corresponding hour in the evening. Use the following prayer before each meal during the entire quarter:

->PRAYER<-

> *I implore You, O Great and Powerful ADONAI, Master of all Spirits! I beseech You, O ELOHIM! I implore You, O JEHOVAH! O Great ADONAI, I give unto You my soul, my heart, my internal organs, my hands, my feet, my desires, my entire being! O Great ADONAI, deign to be favorable toward me! So be it. Amen.*

Then eat your meal. Undress as seldom and sleep as little as possible during the whole of this period, but meditate continually on your undertaking, and center all your hopes in the infinite goodness of the Great Adonai. <gloss>1</gloss>

The *Grimoire of Honorius*, which is intended exclusively for members of the clergy <term>Holy Orders</term>, restricts the period of fasting to three days, as will be seen later on. It makes no condition of sexual abstinence <term>continence</term> for those who are already devoted to a life of celibacy. The *Grimorium Verum* prescribes a similar period and adds that the fast should be most severe, and that human society and communication must be avoided as much as possible, but especially the company of women. *The Book of True Black Magic* varies the instruction as follows: <gloss>1</gloss> If the Priest-Exorcist wishes to succeed in the operation, he must—when all other things are prepared—abstain from every labor of soul and body, from excessive eating and drinking, from all luxury, and from all vice. He must meditate for nine complete days before undertaking the work on those things which he is about to put into practice; his assistants—if there are any—must do the same, so that all requirements may be truly fulfilled. Let them begin with the following prayer, which should be recited twice in the night and once in the day.

->PRAYER<-

> *O Lord God Almighty, be favorable toward us, though we are unworthy to lift our eyes to Heaven by reason of the multitude of our offenses! O God all-merciful, Who does not desire the death of a sinner, but rather his true conversion, bestow Your grace on us! O Lord, O God, full of compassion, aid us in this work which we are about to perform, that Your Name may be blessed forever! Amen.* <gloss>2</gloss>

The concluding days of the fast should be additionally

### § 4. Concerning the External Preparation of the Operator, and firstly Concerning Ritual Washing <term>Ablution</term>

The ritual of purification <term>lustration</term><note>A ceremonial washing or cleansing used to symbolize internal purity.</note>, being the sacramental sign of inward cleanliness, is prescribed in all magical ceremonies and is equally important in the Goetic art <term>Goëtic art</term><note>A branch of magic dealing specifically with the invocation of spirits or demons.</note>. ritual washing <note>original: "ablution"</note> itself becomes symbolic. When the operator rises in the morning, during the preparatory three-day period <note>original: "triduum"</note> prescribed by the *Grimorium Verum* <note>The "True Grimoire," a 18th-century handbook of black magic</note>, his hands and face must be sponged with a clean white cloth, using water which has been previously exorcised according to the instructions of the Ritual <gloss>1</gloss> and reciting the

### PREPARATORY PRAYER

> *Lord God ADONAY <note>A Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord," commonly used in ceremonial magic</note>, Who has formed man out of nothing in Your own image and likeness, and me also, unworthy sinner as I am, deign, I pray You, to bless and sanctify this water, so that it may be healthy for my body and soul, and that all delusion may depart from me. O Lord God, Almighty and Ineffable, Who led forth Your people from the land of Egypt and caused them to pass dry-shod over the Red Sea! Grant that I may be cleansed by this water from all my sins and may appear innocent before You. Amen.*

The *Book of True Black Magic* <note>original: *Le Livre de la Vraie Noire Magie*, a variant of the Key of Solomon</note> observes that the bath is absolutely necessary for the Magical Art, <gloss>2</gloss> and that it must be taken on the final day of the fast. This occurs when the exorcist has been cleansed from all sins and has written—using the ritual pen <note>original: "pen of the Art"</note> upon untainted <note>original: "virgin"</note> paper or parchment—all the conjurations and exorcisms required for the practice, and has performed all the other preparatory ceremonies. From the crown of his head to the soles of his feet, he must purify himself with warm exorcised water, saying as he does so: *O Lord Adonay, Who has formed me in Your image and in Your likeness, deign to bless and sanctify this water, so that it may become for me the salvation of my soul and body, and that no wickedness may ever find a place within me. . . .* The Psalms *The Lord is my light* <note>original: *Dominus illuminatio mea*; Psalm 27</note> and *The fool has said in his heart* <note>original: *Dixit insipiens in corde suo*; Psalm 14</note> should be added. <gloss>1</gloss> Then he should dry himself.

The following prayer must be repeated—five times during the day and four times during the night—for a period of three days: ASTROSCHIO, ASATH, from the sacred <note>original: *à sacra*</note> BEDRIMUBAL, FELUT, ANABOTOS, SERABILEM, SERGEN, GEMEN, DOMOS. <note>These are "barbarous names" or words of power, common in ancient and medieval magic, often consisting of corrupted Greek, Hebrew, or invented syllables.</note> *O Lord God, Who are seated above the heavens, Who look upon the depths, grant me, I pray You, that those things which I conceive in my mind may also be executed by me, through You, O Great God, Who live and reign for ever and ever! Amen.*

### § 5. Concerning the Physical Preparation of the Magician, and Secondly Concerning the Ritual Robes <note>original: "vestments"</note>

The use of special clothing in magical ceremonies follows logically from the religious nature attributed to these operations. Peter of Abano <note>Pietro d'Abano (c. 1250–1316), an Italian philosopher and suspected magician</note> recommends wearing a priest's garment if possible, which may be understood as an alb <note>A long white linen robe worn by Christian clergy</note>. <gloss>2</gloss>

*The Book of Black Magic*, following the *Key of Solomon* and representing the Jewish rite, prescribes an outer robe similar to that worn by the Levites <note>Members of the Hebrew tribe of Levi who assisted the priests in the Temple</note>, with the following symbols <note>original: "characters"</note> embroidered in red silk upon the chest:

<image-desc>A series of esoteric sigils and characters arranged in two horizontal rows. The symbols include glyphs resembling a stylized 'B' with a crossbar, various lines with vertical strikes, a rectangular box containing vertical lines, and several cursive flourishes, loops, and script-like characters, including one that resembles the numeral '6'.</image-desc>

All clothing, even down to the shirt, must be made of linen, <gloss>1</gloss> and when putting them on, these words should be recited: ANTON, AMATOR, EMITES, THEODONIEL, PONCOR, PAGOR, ANITOR; <gloss>2</gloss>

> *By the power of these most holy Angelic Names, I clothe myself, O Lord, in my Sabbath garments, so that I may fulfill, even to their conclusion, all things which I desire to accomplish through You, Most Holy ADONAY <note>Hebrew for "Lord"</note>, Whose kingdom and rule endure forever and ever. Amen.*

The shoes and hat should be made of white leather, bearing the same symbols <gloss>3</gloss> written in <term>cinnabar</term> <note>A bright red mineral, mercury sulfide, used as a pigment</note> diluted with gum-water, using the ritual pen <note>original: "pen of the Art"</note>. The following names must be written around the hat: <gloss>4</gloss> JEHOVA <note>The personal name of God in the Hebrew Bible</note>, at the back; ADONAY, on the right hand; ELOY <note>A form of "Elohim," Hebrew for "God"</note>, on the left; and GIBOR <note>Hebrew for "Mighty"</note>, at the front. <gloss>5</gloss>

# CHAPTER II

## The Initial Rites and Ceremonies

### § 1. Concerning the Virtues of the Planets

THE *Key of Solomon the King* is the only standard Magical Ritual that organizes the operations of *Magical Art* according to a formal system of assigning certain hours of the day and night to the rule and influence of specific planets. Meanwhile, the *Book of True Black Magic* is the only Goëtic <note>Refers to "Goetia," a practice involving the summoning of spirits or demons</note> Grimoire that closely follows the *Clavicle* in this and other matters. 1 The instructions provided are, however, extremely confusing. The standard assignment of the seven days of the week to the seven planets is used in both cases and is presented in the Grimoire as follows: 2

| | | | |
|---|---|---|---|
| Solday <note>Likely a corruption of "Sabbaday" or a specific ritual term for Saturday</note> | = Saturn | = ♄ | = Saturday |
| Zedex <note>From the Hebrew "Tzedek," meaning Jupiter</note> | = Jupiter | = ♃ | = Thursday |
| Madime <note>From the Hebrew "Madim," meaning Mars</note> | = Mars | = ♂ | = Tuesday |
| Zemen <note>Likely a corruption of "Shemesh," the Hebrew word for the Sun</note> | = Sun <note>original: "Sol"</note> | = ☉ | = Sunday |

<summary>This page concludes the footnotes regarding ritual attire and begins Chapter II, discussing how magical operations are timed based on the planetary associations of the days of the week.</summary>

<keywords>Key of Solomon, Clavicle, Goëtic Grimoire, pseudo-Agrippa, planetary virtues, ritual timing, astrology, Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sol</keywords>

--- Page 123 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary influences, moving from the general concept of planetary virtues to the specific, mathematical methods used by grimoires to calculate "planetary hours" for magical operations.</meta>

| | | | |
|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| Hogos | = Venus | = ♀ | = Friday. |
| Cocao | = Mercury | = ☿ | = Wednesday. |
| Zeveac | = Moon | = ☽ | = Monday. |

However, since the length of the days varies, according to the <term>Grimoire</term>—meaning that the relative duration of day and night changes based on the times of sunrise and sunset—the planetary hours are also of unequal length. For example, if the daylight lasts fifteen hours, you would multiply those fifteen hours by five to find out how many minutes make up one planetary hour for that day. The result is seventy-five, so seventy-five minutes would constitute one planetary hour during that day. The corresponding hours of the night would be nine; multiplying these by five produces forty-five, meaning forty-five minutes would make up the planetary hour at night. This same method may be followed throughout all seasons of the year.

However, in any given twenty-four-hour day, the total number of minutes is 1,440, whereas the calculation above results in 1,530 minutes; the method is therefore logically flawed <note>original: "absurd"</note>. <note>1</note> The system accepted by all authors on <term>Natural Magic</term> <note>A branch of magic dealing with the hidden powers of nature and the stars, rather than the summoning of demons</note>, which corresponds with the sequence of angels described by <term>Peter de Abano</term> <note>A 13th-century physician and astrologer famous for his work on planetary influences</note>, can be found in the following:

<note>Practitioners of ceremonial magic used these tables to determine the precise "planetary hour" for their rituals. They believed that an operation related to love should be performed during the hour of Venus, while an operation for power or justice should occur during the hour of Jupiter. Note that the first hour of the day always corresponds to the planet that rules that specific day (e.g., the Sun rules Sunday).</note>

<summary>This page contains reference tables for the planetary hours of the day (hours 8-12) and the night (hours 1-12), showing which celestial body governs each specific time for every day of the week.</summary>
<keywords>planetary hours, astrology, Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, Saturday, Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn, ceremonial magic</keywords>

--- Page 125 ---
<meta>This page follows the "Table of Planetary Hours," explaining how these astrological timings are applied to specific magical rituals and intentions as described in grimoires like the Key of Solomon.</meta>

It will be seen from this table that there is a recurring rule of the planets in unbroken succession through the whole week, after which the sequence starts again in the same order. Each planet rules the first and the eighth of the day hours, and the third and the tenth of the night hours of the day assigned to that planet. These constitute the planetary hours.

The *Book of Black Magic*, still following and confusing the statements of the *Clavicle* <gloss>1</gloss> <note>The *Clavicula Salomonis*, or "Key of Solomon," is one of the most famous handbooks of magic from the Renaissance.</note>, states that the hours of Saturn, Mars, and Venus are good for communicating with spirits. The hour of Saturn serves for invoking souls in hell and to get news of those who have died naturally. The souls of those who have been killed should be invoked in the hour and also on the day of Mars. It adds that experiments performed faithfully and with great care, observing everything that is laid down, will always be successful; however, to fail in even the smallest part will ruin the perfection of the whole.

The hours of Saturn and of Mars are also good for preparation on those days when they are in <term>conjunction</term> <note>When two celestial bodies appear in the same part of the sky.</note> with the Moon, or indeed with one another. <gloss>2</gloss> In an opposing or <term>square aspect</term> <note>original: "contrary or quadrate aspect." These are astrological positions (90 or 180 degrees apart) often associated with conflict or tension.</note>, they are good for rituals involving hatred, lawsuits, enemies, discord, and so forth. The hours of the Sun, Jupiter, and Venus—especially their specific planetary hour—are favorable to both ordinary and extraordinary experiments not included in those already mentioned. 

The hours of the Moon are especially suited to the summoning of spirits, works of <term>necromancy</term> <note>Magic involving communication with the dead or the use of corpses.</note>, and the finding of stolen goods; but the Moon must be positioned in an Earth sign—that is to say, in conjunction with Mercury for love, favors, and invisibility. For works of hatred and discord, it should be in a sign belonging to the <term>fire signs</term> <note>original: "fiery triplicity"—Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius.</note>. For extraordinary experiences, it should be in a <term>water sign</term> <note>original: "aquatic sign"—Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces.</note>. Should, however, the observation of these rules seem too difficult, "do this only": see that the waxing Moon is at an equal number of degrees with the Sun; it is then very good for performing the experiments mentioned above. When the full Moon is in <term>opposition</term> <note>Positioned directly opposite the Sun in the sky.</note> to the Sun, it is excellent for warlike, riotous, and discordant experiments; in its last quarter, it favors works which deal directly with operations of destruction and ruin. The best time for the rituals of death and invisibility is when the Moon is almost entirely dark.

When the Moon is in conjunction with the Sun (the New Moon), nothing must be undertaken, because it is an unfortunate time and all things fail then. But when it is in its crescent period, filled with light, any experiments may be prepared and any writings and operations accomplished, especially for conversing with spirits. It must, however, be the day of Mercury and in the hour of that planet, with the Moon being in an earth or air sign, as said above, and in an equal number of degrees with the Sun. <gloss>1</gloss>

151:1 It offers no comparison to the system set forth in the *Clavicle* <note>original: "Clavicle," referring to the *Clavicula Salomonis* or "Key of Solomon."</note>, nor can it be definitely traced to any magical authority known to me.

152:1 In the version that follows, which condenses and summarizes the original, there is an attempt to organize them and make them consistent, if not entirely intelligible.

152:2 *The Key of Solomon* says that the hours of Saturn and of Mars, and also the days in which the Moon is in <term>conjunction</term> <note>When two celestial bodies appear in the same part of the sky.</note> with these planets, are excellent for experiments of hatred, enmity, and discord. We shall see in what sense this collection of wicked nonsense can be regarded as being "on God's side," as its improbable English editor would undoubtedly wish us to believe.

153:1 The attributions in the original text are not only wrong throughout this portion, but they seem to involve impossible planetary positions. They have been partially corrected in accordance with the *Key of Solomon*.

---

### § 2. A General Instruction concerning the Instruments required for the Art

Most <term>Goëtic</term> <note>Related to "Goëtia," a term for magic involving the invocation of spirits, often specifically "low" or "black" magic.</note> rituals specify with considerable detail the instruments required for the operations. In these respects—though with variations unique to themselves that distinguish them from one another—they follow the authority of the *Key of Solomon*. Peter de Abano <note>A 13th-century physician and astrologer whose name was often attached to later magical texts.</note> tells us nothing concerning them, nor does the <term>pseudo-Agrippa</term> <note>A reference to the *Fourth Book of Occult Philosophy*, a famous text falsely attributed to the philosopher Henry Cornelius Agrippa.</note>, with the single exception of the sword; following a physical logic, the sword seems to be universal in practical magic. In the <term>grimoires</term> <note>Books of magic spells and instructions.</note>, however, though it is sometimes the first weapon mentioned, its position is relatively insignificant, as the knife is a more convenient instrument. Generally speaking, there is little in the literature to justify the complex modern elaborations of Éliphas Lévi and of Christian <note>Referring to Paul Christian, a 19th-century French writer on the occult.</note>.

Among the necessary items mentioned by the *Book of True Black Magic* are the sword, the staff, the rod, the <term>lancet</term> <note>A small, sharp surgical knife.</note>, the *arctrave* or hook, the *bolline* or sickle, the needle, the poniard, a white-handled knife, and another knife with a black handle used to draw the ritual circle. The most important tool to make is the one called the *bolline*; it must be forged on the day and in the hour of Jupiter, using a small piece of unused steel. Heat it three times in the fire and extinguish it in the blood of a mole mixed with the juice of the <term>pimpernel</term> <note>A flowering plant (Anagallis arvensis) often used in herbal magic.</note>. <sup>1</sup> This should be done when the Moon is at its full light and in its proper course. On the same day and in the hour of Jupiter, fit a horn handle <sup>2</sup> to the steel.

> *I conjure you, O form of this instrument, by the authority of God the Father Almighty, by the power of Heaven and the stars, by the power of the Angels, by that of the elements, by that of stones and herbs, and likewise by the power of snowstorms, thunder, and winds: that you receive all power for the performance of those things we are concerned with completing—entirely without trickery, falsehood, or deception—by the command of God, Creator of the ages and Emperor of the Angels. Amen.*

The following salutations must then be pronounced over the instrument: *O Lord, my God, in You have I trusted; I will praise You, O Lord, with my whole heart; As the deer longs for the water springs,* <gloss>1</gloss> <note>original: "Domine, Deus meus, in te speravi; Confitebor tibi, Domine, in toto corde meo: Quemadmodum desiderat cervus ad fontes aquarum." Let the Moon be in a favorable phase, increasing in light and moving swiftly, with the operation beginning in the first hour of Venus and ending at the ninth hour of the same day. Fit the knife with a handle of white wood cut with a single stroke of a new sword or knife, and on this handle engrave the following characters:

The poniard <note>A long, slender dagger.</note> or stiletto and the lancet should be made in the same manner on the day and in the hour of Mercury <note>In planetary magic, Mercury rules Wednesday and is associated with communication, speed, and intelligence.</note>. The steel should be quenched <note>original: "extinguished"</note> in the blood of a mole and the juice of the herb Mercury <note>Likely *Mercurialis annua*, a plant associated with the god Mercury.</note>. They should be fitted with horn handles, <note>1</note> shaped with a new sword in the day and hour of Mercury, and should bear these characters upon the handles:

<image-desc>A sequence of nine magical characters and sigils. From left to right: the numeral 8; the capital letter J; two capital letters S; a symbol resembling the zodiac sign Pisces; a character like a lowercase letter 'm' with a cross at the end of the final stroke; a character like a capital 'T' with a cross on the vertical stem; a closing parenthesis or crescent; the astrological symbol for Mars; and a cross with short cross-bars at the end of each arm.</image-desc>

Fumigate <note>To purify with smoke, usually incense.</note> them as described before, and use them in their proper place as required.

The *Grimorium Verum* <note>original: "True Grimoire," an 18th-century handbook of black magic.</note>, which aims for simplicity, reduces the steel instruments to only three: the knife, the graver <note>An engraving tool.</note>, and the lancet. The first two should be made on the day and in the hour of Jupiter <note>Thursday, associated with authority and growth.</note>, with the waxing Moon in the ascendant; this instruction contradicts the previous authority. When finished, the following Prayer or Conjuration must be recited over each of them. The knife should be large enough to sever the neck of a kid <note>A young goat.</note> at a single blow; it should be fitted with a wooden handle made on the same day and in the same hour, and engraved with magical characters. The instrument should then be sprinkled, fumigated, and kept for use as needed.

### CONJURATION

> I conjure you, O form of this instrument, [Name of tool], by God the Father Almighty; by the power <note>original: "virtue"</note> of Heaven and by all the ruling stars; by the power of the four elements; by that of all stones, all plants, and all animals whatsoever; by the power of hailstorms and winds; to receive such power here that we may

---

<page-num>160</page-num>

> obtain through you the perfect outcome of all our desires, which we also seek to perform without evil and without deception, by God, the Creator of the Sun and the Angels. Amen.

Recite the Seven Penitential Psalms <note>Psalms 6, 32, 38, 51, 102, 130, and 143, traditionally used to express sorrow for sin.</note>, and add these words: DALMALEY, LAMECK, CADAT, PANCIA, VELOUS, MERROE, LAMIDECK, CALDULECH, ANERETON, MITRATON—most pure Angels, be the guardians of these instruments, which are needed for many things.

The lancet, which is mentioned but not described in the *Book of True Black Magic*, should—according to the *Grimorium Verum*—be made in the day and hour of Mercury, while the Moon is waxing. It should be conjured in the same way as the knife and the graver. If assistants accompany the practitioner <note>original: "operator"</note>, each must be provided with his own knife, and the same rule applies.

## § 3. Concerning the Rod and Staff of the Art

The great mystery of practical magic is supposedly centered in the Magic Rod. Éliphas Lévi <note>A 19th-century French occultist who revived interest in ritual magic</note>, who claims to have reconstructed the original ceremonies but seems to have excessively edited his materials, provides a highly dramatic account of its powers and a complicated method for its preparation. For him, it is the symbol of the transmission of the magical priesthood, which has never ceased since the dark origins of pseudo-transcendental science. The practitioner is overwhelmed with warnings about the secrecy that must be maintained regarding it and might well be discouraged by the difficulties of its consecration. Given such assigned importance, it is strange that Pietro d'Abano <note>original: "De Abano," a 13th-century physician and occultist</note> and the author known as pseudo-Agrippa omit all mention of this tremendous instrument, while the *Key of Solomon* dismisses it in a few lines of easy instructions. It appears, however, that both a staff and a rod are necessary, especially in Goetic <term>Rituals specifically concerned with the summoning of demons or lower spirits.</term> operations, though their distinct roles are in no

<note>p. 162</note>

case described. According to the *Book of True Black Magic*, the staff should be made of cane and the wand or rod of hazel wood. Both must be "virgin"—that is, having no branches or offshoots. <gloss>1</gloss> They must be cut and trimmed on the day and in the hour of the Sun <note>Sunday</note>, while the following characters must be inscribed upon the staff, but on the day and in the hour of Mercury <note>Wednesday</note>: <gloss>2</gloss>—

<image-desc>A horizontal line of ten magical or alchemical characters. From left to right: a script-like 'J', a square with a cross inside (⊞), a character resembling a 'Z' with a crossbar (Ƶ), a character like a 'Y' with a curved stem and crossbar, a hooked 'J' shape, and five characters resembling stylized vertical strokes or Roman numeral ones (I) with varying serifs.</image-desc>

<summary>This page discusses the preparation and symbolism of the Magic Rod and Staff, noting the conflicting instructions found in various grimoires and the dramatic claims made by later occultists like Éliphas Lévi.</summary>
<keywords>Magic Rod, Staff of the Art, Éliphas Lévi, Key of Solomon, Goetic operations, ritual tools, hazel wand, virgin wood, planetary hours</keywords>

--- Page 132 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the preparation of the ritual Rod and Staff, moving from the general requirements to the specific rituals found in the Grimorium Verum and the Grand Grimoire.</meta>

<page-num>163</page-num>

If these items are engraved with the "sacred instrument" <note>original: "sacred instrument," referring to the ritual knife or burin used for inscriptions</note>, it will be even better. In either case, let the following words be recited when the writing is finished:

> *O Lord <note>original: ADONAY</note>, most Holy and most Powerful, grant that you may consecrate and bless this Staff and this Rod, so that they may possess the required virtue, O most Holy Lord, to whom be honor and glory for ever and ever. Amen.*

Lastly, the two instruments should be <term>asperged</term> <note>sprinkled with holy water</note>, <term>fumigated</term> <note>purified with ritual incense</note>, and put away in the silken cloth.

The *Grimorium Verum* <note>The "True Grimoire," a 18th-century handbook of black magic</note> directs the operator to make two wands from wood that has never borne fruit. The first should be cut at a single stroke from an elder tree on the day and in the hour of Mercury <note>In planetary magic, different hours of the day are ruled by specific planets; Mercury governs communication and intelligence</note>. The second should be made of hazel wood, free from buds, and cut in the hour of the Sun. The magical characters which must be engraved upon each were omitted by the printer, but they are supplied—or substitutes are provided—in the modern Italian versions. These characters are the seal of **Frimost** for the first wand, and that of **Klippoth** for the second (see Chapter 3, Section 2). The prayer of consecration does not vary significantly from that of *True Black Magic*, so it does not need to be repeated here. Asperging and fumigating are prescribed, just as in the previous case.

<page-num>164</page-num>

The *Grand Grimoire* <note>One of the most famous and "dark" of the magic manuals, focusing on summoning spirits</note> devotes an entire chapter to the true composition of the "Mysterious Wand," also known as the "Destroying" or "Blasting Rod." It mentions no other instrument and attributes all power in diabolical evocations to it. It seems that this text provided Éliphas Lévi with the first hint of his even more potent *Verendum* <note>Latin: "that which is to be feared/revered"</note>, though Lévi’s version may be interpreted allegorically. 

On the eve of the great enterprise, says this ritual, you must go in search of a wand or rod of wild hazel that has never borne fruit; its length should be nineteen and a half inches. When you have found a wand of the required form, do not touch it with anything but your eyes. Let it stay there until the next morning, which is the day of the operation. Then, you must cut it at the exact moment the sun rises. Strip it of its leaves and smaller branches, if there are any, using the knife of sacrifice stained with the blood of a victim. Start cutting it as the sun first rises over the horizon, and pronounce the following words:

> *I beseech You, O Great Lord, God, Ariel, and Jehovah <note>original: ADONAY, ELOIM, ARIEL and JEHOVAM</note>, to be favorable toward me, and to endow this Wand which I am cutting with the power and virtue of the rods of Jacob, of Moses, and of the mighty Joshua! I also beseech You, O Great Lord, God, Ariel, and Jehovah, to infuse into this Rod the whole strength of Samson, the righteous wrath of Emmanuel, and the thunders of mighty Zariatnatmik, who will avenge the names of men at the Day of Judgment! Amen.*

Having pronounced these "sublime and terrifying words," and still keeping your eyes turned toward the rising sun, you may finish cutting your rod, and may then carry it away.

<keywords>Magic Wand, Blasting Rod, Grimorium Verum, Grand Grimoire, Adonay, Consecration, Ritual Tools, Hazel wood, Elder wood</keywords>

...take it to your home. You must next go in search of a piece of ordinary wood and shape its two ends to match those of the actual ritual rod. Take this piece to a blacksmith, who shall weld the steel blade of the sacrificial knife into two pointed caps and attach them to those ends. Once this is done, you may return home and, with your own hands, attach the steel caps to the joints of the genuine rod. Afterward, you must obtain a piece of loadstone <note>a naturally magnetized mineral</note> and magnetize the steel ends while reciting the following words:

*By the great ADONAY, ELOIM, ARIEL, and JEHOVAM, I command you to join with and attract all substances which I desire, by the power of the sublime ADONAY, ELOIM, ARIEL, and JEHOVAM. I command you, by the opposition of fire and water, to separate all substances, just as they were separated on the day of the world's creation. Amen.*

Finally, you should rejoice in the honor and glory of the sublime Lord <note>original: Adonay</note>, being convinced that you possess a most priceless Treasure of Light. <gloss>1</gloss>

# § 4. Concerning the Pen and Ink of the Art

Preliminary magical ceremonies seem to have one primary goal: the personal consecration <note>the act of making an object holy or dedicated to a ritual purpose</note> of every item—whether large or small, essential or occasional—involved in the various rituals. The pen and ink used to write sacred symbols and names...

<summary>This page concludes the specific instructions for creating and magnetizing a ritual Magic Rod and begins a new section on the consecration of writing materials like pens and ink.</summary>
<keywords>Magic Rod, loadstone, Adonay, Key of Solomon, hazel wood, ritual tools, consecration, planetary magic, Agla, Tetragrammaton</keywords>

--- Page 134 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific instructions for preparing ritual tools, transitioning from the construction of the rod to the preparation of the writing instruments—specifically the quill and ink-well—required for drawing magical sigils and writing conjurations.</meta>

...the materials upon which they are written must both be specially prepared. Take a new quill, says the *Grimorium Verum* <note>The "True Grimoire," an 18th-century book of magic</note>; sprinkle it with holy water <note>original: asperge</note> and perfume it with incense <note>original: fumigate</note> in the same manner as the other instruments. When you hold it in your hand to shape it, say the following words: ABABALOY, SAMOY, ESCAVOR, LORD <note>original: Adonay</note>: *I have expelled all illusion from this pen, so that it may effectively retain within it the power <note>original: virtue</note> necessary for all things used in this Art, both for ritual operations and for symbols <note>original: characters</note> and conjurations. Amen.*

In the same way, an ink-well <note>original: horn</note> must be bought on the day and in the hour of Mercury <note>In planetary magic, the hours of Mercury (Wednesday) are considered ideal for matters of writing, communication, and intellect</note>. In that same hour, the following names of God must be written around it: JOD, HE, VAU, HE <note>The four letters of the Tetragrammaton, the sacred name of God</note>, METATRON, JOD, <gloss>1</gloss> HOLY <note>original: Cados</note>, <gloss>2</gloss> GOD <note>original: Eloym</note>, <gloss>3</gloss> OF HOSTS <note>original: Sabaoth</note>. It must then be filled with fresh ink which has been exorcised as follows:

## EXORCISM OF THE INK

> *I exorcise you, creature of Ink <note>In this context, "creature" refers to a created element of nature</note>, by* ANSTON, CERRETON, STIMULATOR, <gloss>4</gloss> LORD <note>original: Adonay</note>, *and by the name of Him Whose one Word created all and can achieve all, so that you shall assist me in my work. May my work be accomplished by my will and fulfilled with the permission of God, Who rules in all things and through all things, everywhere and forever. Amen.*

## BENEDICTION OF THE INK

> *Lord God Almighty, Who rules all and reigns through all eternity, Who performs great wonders through Your creatures, grant to us the grace of Your Holy Spirit by means of this pen. Bless it, sanctify it, and bestow upon it a unique*

---

<page-num>166</page-num>

> *power <note>original: virtue</note>, so that whatever is said, and whatever we desire to do and to write with it, may succeed through You, Most Holy Prince* LORD <note>original: Adonay</note>. *Amen.* <gloss>1</gloss>

Then sprinkle it with holy water, perfume it with incense, and exorcise it.

*The Book of True Black Magic*, on the other hand, directs the practitioner to pluck the third feather from the right wing of a male goose <gloss>2</gloss> and to say while extracting it: ABRACHAY, ARATON, SAMATOY, SCAVER, LORD <note>original: Adonay</note>! *Expel all evil from this feather, so that it may possess full power to write whatever I wish.*

Then shape it with the Knife of the Art—specifically, the one with a white handle; incense it and sprinkle it with holy water also. Lastly, take a new earthen ink-vessel <note>original: cornicle or inkhorn</note>, made on the day and in the hour of Venus <note>The day of Venus is Friday; its hours are often chosen for the physical creation of beautiful or artistic objects</note>, and write these names around it with the exorcised ritual blade <note>original: lancet</note> of the Art: JOD, HE, VAU, HE, METATRON, JAE, <gloss>3</gloss> JAE, JAE, HOLY <note>original: Cados</note>, GOD <note>original: Eloyn</note>, OF HOSTS <note>original: Zevao</note>. <gloss>4</gloss> Then dip the pen into it, pronouncing the following words: *I exorcise you, creature of the feather kind, by* ETERETON, <gloss>5</gloss> *by* STIMULATON <gloss>5</gloss> *and by the name* LORD <note>original: Adonay</note>. *Do aid me in all my works.*
