<meta>This page transitions from the rituals of treasure-hunting and spiritual conjuration to Chapter VIII, which focuses on "Miscellaneous and Minor Processes." # Miscellaneous and Minor Processes

## § 1. Concerning Works of Hatred and Destruction

The following process appears in several manuscript copies of the *Key of Solomon*, but the English editor of that work omitted it. It was likely left out—along with others—under the assumption that it was a Goëtic <term>Goëtic: Relating to Goetia, a type of magic involving the invocation of demons or "low" spirits.</term> interpolation. However, we have seen that a Goëtic process in a book like the *Key of Solomon* is not necessarily an addition by a later hand. In the case of this particular instance, it is almost certainly an original part of that questionable collection. 

In the second chapter of the first book, we are told that the days and hours of Mars are suitable for overthrowing enemies. Furthermore, the hours of Saturn and Mars—as well as the days when the Moon is in conjunction with those planets—are excellent for experiments of hatred, enmity, quarreling, and discord. In the first chapter of the second book, it states: "As for operations of destruction and desolation, we should put them into execution on the day of Saturn at the first hour, or rather at the eighth or fifteenth hour of the day, and from the first until the eighth hour of the night." 

From these statements, it seems fair to infer that there must have been a section containing directions on how to perform such works. In this case, the *Key of Solomon* is neither better nor worse than any other *Grimoire of Black Magic*—as we already know from its officially accepted sections. Those who defend the *Clavicle* <note>original: "Clavicle"; another name for the "Key of Solomon" (Clavicula Salomonis).</note>—if there are any besides its English editor—will probably cite a passage from Book I, Chapter 8 in its defense. In that passage, the author offers his secrets only on the express condition that they shall not be used to ruin or destroy one's neighbor. 

However, even when we allow for this requirement, we should remember that similar warnings are found even in purely Goëtic rituals. There is a further test to determine if the following process is authentic: its wording. The style corresponds closely with the general style of the *Clavicle*. It uses loose and general language to cover several types of experiments. If anyone is foolish or overly serious enough to want to test this, they can compare it to Book I, chapters 15, 16, and 17, where the same characteristics are found.

The process itself is as follows: 

Experiments upon enemies may be performed in several ways. However, whether you use waxen images or some other instrument, you must diligently and faithfully observe the details of each. Should the specific day and hour fail you, proceed as previously instructed and prepare the image or instrument for this purpose in the proper order and manner. Fumigate with the appropriate perfumes, and if writing is required on the image, let it be done with the needle or stylus of the art, as mentioned before. Next, recite the following words once over the image:

> CONSUMER, DESTROYER, TEMPTER, TROUBLER <note>original Latin: "VSOR, DILAPIDATORE, TENTATORE, SOIGNATORE, DEVORATORE, CONCITORE, ET SEDUCTORE." Respectively: User/Consumer, Destroyer, Tempter, Caretaker/Troubler, Devourer, Agitator, and Seducer.</note>, DEVOURER, AGITATOR, AND SEDUCER. O all you ministers and companions, I direct, conjure, constrain, and command you to fulfill this behest willingly; namely, to straightway consecrate this image, which is to be done in the name of...

<summary>This page introduces the rituals of hatred and destruction from the "Key of Solomon," arguing that despite being omitted by some editors, these "black magic" elements are likely original to the text. It begins detailing the ritual process, including the specific planetary hours for Mars and Saturn and the start of a conjuration over a wax image.</summary>

<keywords>Key of Solomon, Clavicle, Black Magic, Goetia, Mars, Saturn, waxen images, rituals of destruction, ceremonial magic, malevolent spirits</keywords>

. . . *so that as the face of the one is turned away from the other, they may never look upon each other again.*

Deposit the image in some place filled with foul odors, especially those associated with Mars, such as sulfur and asafoetida <term>Asafoetida: A pungent herbal resin known for its strong, unpleasant smell, used here to attract negative planetary influences.</term>. Let it remain there for one night, having properly sprinkled it <note>original: "asperged"</note>, while observing the correct hour and time. Do the same when the experiment is performed using symbols and names, by touching the lovers with words, or by any other method. However, when the experiment involves giving someone something to eat, it must be performed on the day and hour specifically designated for this work. Once all things are prepared, place them before you and say: 

"Where are you, **Marker, Burner, Destroyer, and Biter; Cutter, Devourer, Seducer, and Sower** <note>original: "SOIGNATORE, USORE, DILAPIDATORE, and DENTORE; CONCISORE, DIVORATORE, SEDUCTORE, and SEMINATORE"</note>? You who sow discord, where are you? You who instill hatred and spread enmity, I conjure you by Him who created you for this ministry to fulfill this work, so that whenever **N.** [naming the person] shall eat such things, or touch them in any way, he shall never be at peace."

Then, give whatever you wish to the designated person, but ensure it is done in the hour of Saturn or the hour of Mars, observing all things necessary for such experiments.

**299:1** This implies that the experiment is intended for the magical satisfaction of a rival. However, it will be seen that there is some confusion, as the prayer that follows will demonstrate.

## § 2. Concerning Experiments of Lust <note>original: "Venereal Experiments"</note>

We have seen that the sorcerer of the Middle Ages was usually impoverished and desperate; therefore, he craved treasures. He was usually despised, and so he longed for mastery, for the prestige of mystery, and the power of strange arts. He was usually lonely and lustful, and thus he sought, through spells and potions <note>original: "philtres"</note>, to compel the desire of women. To be rich in worldly goods, to trample on one's enemies, and to gratify the desires of the flesh—these are the goals, expressed and achieved in various ways, of most ceremonial magic. Consequently, the ritual books abound in experiments regarding lust.

In works such as the *Grimorium Verum*, there is no attempt to hide their illegal and immoral nature. In the *Key of Solomon*, it is disguised, but the process for "obtaining favor and love" found in that work is not truly a more lawful experiment than the one titled "**To make a girl come and find you, no matter how virtuous she may be**" <note>original: "Pour faire venir une Fille vous trouver, si sage qu'elle soit"</note>. The *Key of Solomon* is secretive while the Grimoires are blunt; the former promises the fulfillment of the magician’s desire without defining it, while the others are...

<summary>This page concludes a ritual for causing hatred between individuals and begins an analysis of "Venereal Experiments," arguing that the primary motivations of medieval sorcerers were wealth, power, and the gratification of lust.</summary>
<keywords>magic of hatred, discord rituals, Mars, Saturn, Venereal Experiments, Grimorium Verum, Key of Solomon, sorcerer motivations, love spells</keywords>

--- Page 242 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of "Venereal Experiments" or love magic. It specifically contrasts the methods found in the "Key of Solomon" with those in "The Book of True Black Magic," providing a detailed ritual involving wax images, the invocation of the four demon kings of the cardinal directions, and the use of magical powders to compel a person's affection or presence.</meta>

...explicit and particular. It is also true that while the one has recourse to Adonai <term>Adonai: A Hebrew name for God, frequently used in ceremonial magic to appeal to divine authority.</term> and implores his assistance, the others invoke the powers of Hell.

*The Book of True Black Magic* <note>original: "Le Veritable Magie Noire," a French grimoire based on the Key of Solomon.</note> for once ignores the experience of the *Key of Solomon* <term>original: "Clavicle," from the Latin *Clavicula Salomonis*, the most famous handbook of Renaissance magic.</term> and substitutes the following:

### *Concerning the Ritual of Love*

Whatever person is the object of this ritual, whether man or woman, it is necessary when performing it to observe the proper day and hour. Note further that it is performed by means of an image made of wax or another suitable material. Before forming it, say the following words over the wax: NOGA, JES, ASTROPOLIM, ASMO, COCCAV, BERMONA, TENTATOR, SOIGNATOR—

> I summon you, ministers of love and lust <note>original: "incontinence"</note>, by Him who has condemned you to hell! Consecrate this wax in a proper manner, so that it may acquire the desired power, by the Power of the Most Holy Adonai, Who lives and reigns forever and ever. Amen.

The image must then be formed, and suitable characters inscribed on it with the male goose-quill of the Art <note>A ritual pen prepared according to specific magical instructions.</note>. Afterward, it must be purified with smoke <note>original: "fumigated"</note> while reciting the following words:

> O you, most Powerful King Paimon, whose absolute rule and reign is in the western quarter! O you, Egim, most strong King, whose empire is in the cold region of the North! O you, Asmodeus, who governs in the South! O you, Amaymon, most noble King and monarch of the Eastern world, whose reign began in ancient times and will endure to the end of time! I invoke and beseech you by Him Who spoke and it was, Whose sole word has created all things, Whom all creatures obey; by the seat of His Majesty; by His Will and His Name; by Him Who was before the ages and has created the ages; Whose Name is written with the four letters YOD, HE, VAV, HE <term>The Tetragrammaton: The four-letter Hebrew name of God, often considered the most powerful word in magic.</term>; by the enchantments and power thereof; and by all the sacred Names of the Creator; I conjure you to consecrate this image and grant that it may acquire power according to our desire, by the Most Holy Name Adonai, the Power of which is without beginning or end.

This being done, perform the Conjuration of the image. If the woman or man does not appear, place the image beneath the pillow of your bed, and before three days pass you shall behold marvelous things. Neither chains nor the earth itself shall stop the desired person from coming to you to fulfill your purpose, with nothing being excepted.

But if the goal of the ritual is simply to find the beloved person, place the image under the door through which he or she must pass. In either case, a powder must be made and cast upon the person, or given to them in their food or drink. The proper timing of the hours, the materials, and the instruments must be faithfully observed, and the Spirits must be summoned by name, saying also:

> I summon and constrain you, you Devils, who have the power to disturb the hearts of men and women! By Him Who has created you from nothing and by this image, I conjure you this night into my presence, so that I may have the power to compel whomsoever I will to love me, whether male or female.

<summary>This page details a specific love ritual from "The Book of True Black Magic," involving the creation of a wax image and the invocation of the four demon kings—Paimon, Egim, Asmodeus, and Amaymon—to compel a person's love or presence.</summary>
<keywords>love magic, wax image, Paimon, Egim, Asmodeus, Amaymon, Adonai, Tetragrammaton, grimoire, True Black Magic</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the ritual for "Venereal Experiments" or love magic. After preparing wax figures and invoking various spirits, the text concludes the instructions for the previous ritual and introduces a new process from the *Grimorium Verum* (The True Grimoire) for summoning supernatural visitors to one's room.</meta>

When the characters and images have been prepared, say over them: *I summon you, O ANAËL, DONQUEL, THELIEL, Princes of love, and all your ministers, who have power to fill with fervor the hearts of men and women, and to kindle the fire of love! I summon you by Him Who is seated upon the Cherubim and guards the abysses, by Him Who makes the world to tremble, Whom all creatures obey! Grant that these characters and figures may possess this power <note>original: "virtue"</note>, so that this man or woman may love me, may desire me and thirst for my love, and that it shall be impossible for him or her to love any person except me.*

Place the image in a vessel for one night, perform the ritual on the day and in the hour appropriate to it, and you shall behold a wonder.

The compiler of the *Grimorium Verum* <note>original: "The True Grimoire," a 18th-century handbook of black magic attributed to Alibeck the Egyptian.</note>, who seems to have selected almost whiteout fail the most bizarre methods, instructs his pupils:

### HOW TO CAUSE THE APPEARANCE OF THREE LADIES OR THREE GENTLEMEN IN ONE'S ROOM AFTER SUPPER

#### § 1. Preparation

Abstain for three days from "hidden offices" <term>hidden offices: A common euphemism in magical texts for sexual activity or secret ritual labors.</term>, and then your spirit shall be strengthened. On the fourth day, you shall cleanse and prepare your chamber as soon as it is morning, immediately after dressing, while fasting entirely. But see that you do it in such a way that the room will not be disturbed during the remainder of the day. Note that there must be no wall hangings, nor anything set crosswise, no tapestries, no hanging clothes, hats, bird-cages, bed curtains, etc. Above all, let the furnishings <note>original: "appointments"</note> be clean in every respect.

#### § 2. Ceremony

After supper, go in secret to your chamber, which was prepared as described above. Light a good fire; place a white cloth on the table, around which you must set three chairs. Before each chair, upon the

<page-num>303</page-num>

table, let there be a wheaten roll and a glass full of fresh clear water. Lastly, draw up a chair and settee to the side of the bed and retire to rest, uttering the following

### CONJURATION

> *Besticitum, comfort, come to me; let Creon turn, Creon, Creon, singer of the praise of the Almighty and do not lie. Let the higher writing stand, praising all your power from the mountain, and may my enemies be cast down before you and me, granting that which may happen in sincerity.* <note>original: "Besticitum consolatio, veni ad me vertat Creon, Creon, Creon, cantor laudem omnipotentis et non commentur. Stat superior carta bient laudem omviestra principiem da montem et inimicos meos ô prostantis vobis et mihi dantes que passium fieri sincisibus." This is "barbarous" or corrupt Latin, often used in grimoires for its sound rather than its grammatical accuracy.</note>

<summary>This page concludes a love ritual using wax images and introduces a bizarre ceremony from the *Grimorium Verum* intended to summon three supernatural visitors to a clean, specifically prepared room.</summary>
<keywords>Anaël, Donquel, Theliel, Grimorium Verum, love magic, summoning ritual, ritual purity, Besticitum, Creon, conjuration</keywords>

--- Page 244 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the ritual to summon three supernatural visitors (either ladies or gentlemen) to one's room. The previous page established the physical preparation of the room and the requirement of fasting and celibacy.</meta>

The three people, after arriving, will rest near the fire, eating and drinking, and finally thanking the person who has hosted them. If a young woman performs this ceremony, three gentlemen will appear; if a man performs it, three young women will arrive. These three people will draw lots among themselves to decide which one of them will stay with you. If the practitioner is a man, the woman who wins the draw will sit in the armchair you placed by the bed. She will stay and talk with you until midnight, at which point she will leave with her companions without needing to be formally dismissed. 

As for the other two, they will stay by the fire while the third entertains you. While she remains, you may ask her about any art, science, or any subject whatsoever, and she will provide a clear and definite answer immediately. You can also ask her if she knows of any hidden treasure; she will tell you its location and the exact time it should be removed. She will even go to that location with her companions to protect you from the attacks of the Infernal Spirits who might be guarding it. Upon leaving, she will give you a ring. Wearing this ring on your finger will make you lucky in gambling <note>original: "play"</note>. If you place the ring on the finger of any woman or girl, you will immediately <meta>p. 304</meta> have your way with her. However, you must remember to leave your window open so that the spirits can enter.

This ceremony can be repeated as often as the practitioner desires.

One must admit that the ritual described above offers a great reward for very little effort in preparation; for this reason, it is very popular among the authors of Grimoires <term>Grimoires: Textbooks of magic, typically providing instructions on how to summon supernatural entities or create magical objects.</term>. The same can be said for the following ritual, which is found in the supplements of the *Grand Grimoire*, the *Grimorium Verum* <note>original: "The True Grimoire"</note>, the *Grimoire of Honorius*, and most minor collections. It is described as a demonstration of the wonderful power of "Superior Intelligences." However, the actual nature of this "superiority" can be guessed from the title.

### TO MAKE A GIRL SEEK YOU OUT, NO MATTER HOW CAUTIOUS SHE MAY BE

<image-desc>A magical sigil consisting of a vertical line topped with a small six-pointed star. A horizontal cross-bar intersects the vertical line; the left end of the bar terminates in a small circle, and the right end terminates in an arrowhead with a curved line drawn through it. Below this structure is a large circular emblem containing two inner concentric circles. Inside the innermost circle are two calligraphic letters "N. N."</image-desc>

<summary>This page concludes the ritual for summoning three supernatural visitors who provide knowledge, protection, and a magical ring for luck and seduction. Waite then introduces a new "Superior Intelligence" spell designed to compel a woman to visit the practitioner.</summary>
<keywords>Grimoires, Grand Grimoire, Grimorium Verum, Grimoire of Honorius, Infernal Spirits, Superior Intelligences, sigil, love magic, summoning, treasure hunting</keywords>

--- Page 245 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of rituals for summoning spirits or influencing others. The previous page concluded a ritual involving three supernatural visitors who provide secrets, treasures, and a magical ring for gambling and seduction.</meta>

<page-num>305</page-num>

Whether during the waxing or waning of the Moon, a star must be observed between eleven o'clock and midnight. But before beginning, do as follows. Take a piece of virgin parchment <term>virgin parchment: animal skin prepared for writing that has never been used and is often made from a stillborn or young animal to ensure purity</term>. Write upon it the name of the woman whose presence you desire. The parchment must be shaped as represented in the following figure <note>Waite refers to a diagram that would appear in the original text</note>. On the other side, inscribe these words: *Melchiael, Bareschas*. Then place your parchment on the ground, with the person’s name facing the earth. Set your right foot upon it, while your left knee is bent to the ground. In this position, observe the brightest star in the heavens, holding in your right hand a candle of white wax large enough to last for an hour, and recite the following:

### CONJURATION

> *I greet and command you, O beautiful Moon, O beautiful Star, O bright light which I hold in my hand! By the air which I breathe, by the breath which is within me, by the earth which I touch, I command you; and by all the names of the spirits who are Princes residing within you; by the unutterable Name ON <note>original: "ON"; an ancient mystical name for the Divine, sometimes associated with the sun or the Greek word for 'being'</note>, which has created all; by you, O Radiant Angel Gabriel, together with the Prince Mercury, Michiael, and Melchidael! I command you again by all the Divine Names of God, that you send down spirits to obsess, torment, and harass the body, spirit, soul, and five senses of the nature of N. <note>N. serves as a placeholder for the target's name</note>, whose name is written here below, in such a way that she shall come to me and fulfill my will, having no friendship for anyone in the world, and especially not for N., so long as she remains indifferent to me. Thus shall she not endure, thus shall she be obsessed, thus suffer, and thus be tormented. Go then, promptly; go, Melchidael, Baresches, Zazel, Firiel, Malcha, and all those who are with you. I command you by the great living God to fulfill my will, and I, N., do promise to satisfy you duly.*

Having pronounced this conjuration three times, place the candle on the parchment and let it burn. Take the parchment the next morning, put it in your left shoe, and leave it there until the person for whom you have performed the ritual comes to seek you out. You must specify in the Conjuration the day that you desire her to come, and she will not fail.

This process has its disadvantages, and even its difficulties. It seems absurd to suppose that there are Spirit Princes inhabiting the flame of a candle, and the treatment invoked upon the lady is of a violent nature; nor is there any plausible excuse by which the displacement of a rival lover can be justified.

<page-num>306</page-num>

However, it has a touch of the picturesque. Even when making all allowances for the powers that may reside in the phrase *The sower Arepo holds the wheels with effort* <note>original Latin: "Sator, Arepo, Tenet, Opera, Rotas"; a famous magical word square that reads the same in all directions</note>, this ritual does not suffer significantly when compared to the methods found in the *Key of Solomon* <note>original: "Clavicle"; refers to the 'Clavicula Salomonis,' one of the most famous medieval handbooks of magic</note>.

## § 3. Concerning the Experiment of Invisibility

We shall see later on that there are certain processes found in the Grimoires <term>Grimoires: Textbooks of magic, providing instructions on how to create magical objects, summon spirits, or cast spells</term> which do not connect with Black Magic except by their inclusion in these books. There are also...

<summary>This page describes a ritual intended to compel a woman to visit the practitioner by using a parchment, a candle, and a conjuration of celestial spirits. Waite critiques the ritual's absurdity and ethics but notes its "picturesque" quality compared to other famous magical texts.</summary>
<keywords>Moon magic, conjuration, Melchiael, Gabriel, candle ritual, seduction magic, Sator Square, Clavicle of Solomon, grimoires</keywords>

--- Page 246 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of rituals that straddle the line between natural magic and black magic. The author is transitioning from rituals of seduction and gambling to the experiment of invisibility, comparing how different grimoires—from the "Key of Solomon" to the more sinister "Grimorium Verum"—treat the same magical goal.</meta>

<page-num>307</page-num>

...other processes which are not in themselves *Goetic* <term>Goetic: relating to Goetia, a type of magic involving the invocation of spirits or demons, often considered "black magic"</term>, but are objectionable because of the ease with which they can be abused. It is curious to observe how experiments of this kind will appear under a harmless guise in one ritual, but in another, they will bear all the marks of diabolism. The experiment of Invisibility, which we are discussing here, illustrates all these points.

Here, Ceremonial Magic claims to give its practitioners possession of the Ring of Gyges <note>original: "ring of Gyges"; a mythical ring mentioned by the philosopher Plato that granted its owner the power to become invisible</note>. Assuming that the *Key of Solomon* is the oldest of all the rituals, it is there that the formal process first appears. It is accomplished, however, without the use of a ring. Instead, it uses a simple preliminary invocation and an address to *Almiras*, the Master and Chief of Invisibility. Any other necessary items for the specific occasion, such as magical characters and circles, are left to the practitioner's discretion. There is also a secondary process involving a wax image; it appears in one manuscript copy and is included by the English editor. The person who has properly made and consecrated this image is supposed to become invisible while carrying it. 

If we now turn to the *Book of True Black Magic*, we find the first experiment adapted as follows: Before performing the experiment of invisibility, these words must be memorized: 

> SCABOLES, HABRION, ELÆ, ELIMIGIT, GABOLII, SEMITRION, MENTINOBOL, SABANITEUT, HEREMOBOL, CANE, METHÉ, BALUTI, CATEA, TIMEGUEL, BORA, *by the power you hold over us, fulfill this work, so that I may become invisible.* <margin>1. These words must be written with the blood mentioned previously.</margin> <margin>2. The blood mentioned refers to specific ritual preparations described in earlier chapters of the grimoire.</margin> 

The words above must be written with the blood mentioned earlier, and the following Conjuration recited: 

> *O You Spirits of Invisibility, I conjure and constrain you immediately and at once to consecrate this experiment, so that, surely and without trickery, I may go invisible. Furthermore, I conjure you by Lucifer, your prince, by the obedience which you owe to him, and by the power of God, to aid me immediately by consecrating this experiment, without loss of my body or my soul. So be it, so be it, so be it.*

Prepare everything required for this experiment with proper solemnity and diligence, as it is laid down in the chapter dedicated to it: in this way you shall operate with certainty and find the truth. But if you fail to provide any of the necessary things, you will not achieve your desire; for no man enters a town through the walls, but only through the gate.

This is merely a shorter version of the process found in the *Clavicle* <note>original: "Clavicle"; another name for the "Key of Solomon" (from the Latin 'Clavicula')</note>, with the reference to Lucifer added in.

In place of these plain and somewhat meaningless directions, the *Grimorium Verum* provides an extremely curious process. It is at once monstrous and fantastic, echoing the ancient sorceries of Thessaly <note>Thessaly, a region in Greece, was famous in antiquity for its powerful witches and herbal magic</note> and having direct connections with folklore.

->To become Invisible<-

Begin this operation on a Wednesday before the sun rises, having provided yourself with seven black beans. Next, take the head...

<summary>This page compares rituals for invisibility across different magical texts, noting how the "Key of Solomon" uses invocations while the "Book of True Black Magic" adds demonic appeals to Lucifer. It introduces a more macabre, folklore-based invisibility ritual from the "Grimorium Verum" involving black beans.</summary>

<keywords>invisibility, Key of Solomon, Book of True Black Magic, Almiras, Lucifer, Grimorium Verum, Ring of Gyges, Goetic magic, sorcery, ritual blood</keywords>

--- Page 247 ---
<meta>This page continues a gruesome ritual for invisibility involving a human skull and transitions into a more "refined" alchemical method involving a magical ring and planetary alignments.</meta>

...of a dead man. Place one of the beans in his mouth, two in his eyes, and two in his ears. Then, draw upon this head the symbol of the figure that follows here. (*This figure is omitted in all the Grimoires.*) This done, bury the head with the face looking upward toward the sky. Every day for nine days, before sunrise, water it with high-quality brandy. On the eighth day, you will encounter the summoned spirit <note>original: "cited spirit"</note>, who will ask you: "What are you doing?" 

You shall reply: *I am watering my plant.* 

He will then say: *Give me that bottle; I will water it myself.* 

You must refuse him. He will ask you again, but you must persist in declining until he reaches out his hand and shows you the same figure you drew upon the head, suspended from the tips of his fingers. In this case, you may be certain that it is truly the spirit of the head; if you are not careful, another spirit might catch you off guard, which would bring you harm and make your operation useless. Once you have given him your flask, he will water the head and leave. 

On the following day—the ninth day—you shall return and will find your beans are ripe. Take them, place one in your mouth, and then look at yourself in a mirror. If you cannot see your reflection, the ritual has worked. Test the rest of the beans the same way, or they may be tested in the mouth of a child. Any beans that do not grant invisibility must be buried again with the head.

The advantage of occasional invisibility for the sake of forbidden romance <note>original: "illicit affection"</note> seems to have fascinated the editor of the *Little Albert* <note>original: "Little Albert"; refers to the "Petit Albert," a famous 18th-century grimoire of natural and cabalistic magic</note>. He refers to the legend of Gyges' adultery with evident enjoyment when introducing his process for:

### Invisibility by means of a Ring

This important operation must be performed on a Wednesday in springtime, under the influence of Mercury, when that planet is

<page-num>309</page-num>

known to be aligned with other favorable planets, such as the Moon, Jupiter, Venus, or the Sun. Taking high-quality mercury, "fixed" <term>fixed mercury: an alchemical term for mercury that has been solidified or stabilized so it does not evaporate when heated</term> and well-purified, create a large ring from it, sized so that it passes easily over the middle finger of the hand. Let the collet <term>collet: the setting or rim of a ring that holds a gemstone in place</term> be set with a small stone found in a pewit's <term>pewit: also known as a lapwing; its nests were often searched for "stones" in traditional folk magic</term> nest. Around the band of the ring, the following words should be engraved: *Jesus, passing through the midst of them, disappeared.* <note>original: "Jesus passing through the midst of them disappeared"; a reference to the Gospel of Luke 4:30, frequently used in the Middle Ages and Renaissance as a charm for invisibility or protection</note>

Next, place the ring on a palette-shaped plate made of fixed mercury. Prepare the "perfume of mercury" <note>a specific ritual incense associated with the planet Mercury</note> and expose the ring to its smoke three times. Wrap the ring in a small piece of taffeta <note>a crisp, high-end silk fabric</note> matching the color of the planet Mercury. Carry it to the same pewit's nest where the stone was found and leave it there for nine days. When you retrieve it, smoke it with the incense exactly as before. Afterward, keep it very carefully in a small box, also made of fixed mercury, and use it when needed. 

To use the ring, place it on your finger with the stone facing outward; its power will so entrance onlookers that you may be present without being seen. When you no longer wish to be invisible, simply turn the ring so the stone faces inward and close your hand over it.

<summary>This page details two methods for achieving invisibility: a grotesque ritual involving planting beans in a human skull and an alchemical process for creating a magical mercury ring inscribed with biblical text.</summary>
<keywords>invisibility, Little Albert, alchemical ring, fixed mercury, pewit stone, Mercury, planetary magic, necromancy</keywords>

--- Page 248 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of magical rituals for invisibility, shifting from the use of beans and spirits to the creation of magical rings using animal parts and planetary timing.</meta>

But Éliphas Lévi <note>A famous 19th-century French occultist and author</note> claims that the only authors who have written seriously about the ring of Gyges <note>A mythical ring mentioned by Plato that granted its owner invisibility</note> or its equivalents are Porphyry, Iamblichus, and Peter of Apono <note>Pietro d'Abano, a 13th-century physician and astrologer</note>. He argues that their writings are allegorical and that they are actually referring to the Great Magical Secret <note>original: "Great Magical Arcanum"</note>. Regardless, the *Little Albert* <note>The *Petit Albert*, a famous 18th-century grimoire of natural and cabalistic magic</note> provides an alternative method which it claims to derive from these authorities and also from Cornelius Agrippa—who is sometimes called the master of Peter of Apono, despite the fact that they lived centuries apart.

<margin>p. 310</margin>
The first requirement is a tuft of hair taken from the top of a hyena’s head. These hairs must be braided into a ring, which must then be carried, like the previous one, to a lapwing's <note>original: "pewit's"</note> nest and left there, also for a period of nine days. The perfumes of Mercury must be used in the same manner. The person who wears this ring will be invisible and will reappear simply by removing it from his finger.

Éliphas Lévi observes that these directions bring to mind the fable of the "bell of Rodilard" <note>A reference to a La Fontaine fable where mice plan to bell a cat (Rodilard) but find the task impossible</note>, but his criticism is hardly appropriate. The materials are certainly possible to find; one would not even need to catch their own hyena. The real point, which the occultist missed but the ordinary reader might see, is that a hyena’s head has very short hair, and such hairs could not actually be braided. It is not, however, unreasonable that impossible conditions should be required for an impossible object.

307:2 Possibly that of a mole, used in the consecration of the instruments.

309:1 Compare the *Magic Circle of Honorius* <note>A medieval grimoire attributed to Pope Honorius III</note>.

It involves creating a similarly shaped ring made of purified lead, set with the eye of a female ferret that has had only one litter. The words "The Lord appeared to Simon" <note>original: "Apparuit Dominus Simoni"</note> should be engraved around the circumference. It should be made on a Saturday when Saturn is in opposition with Mercury, perfumed three times with the perfume of Saturday, wrapped in a piece of a burial shroud <note>original: "winding-sheet"</note>, and then buried for nine days in a churchyard. After being dug up <note>original: "disinterred"</note> and perfumed again, it is ready for use.

### § 4. Concerning the Hand of Glory

<summary>This page describes methods for creating rings of invisibility using hyena hair and discusses a counter-charm ring made of lead and a ferret's eye. The author highlights the absurdity and practical impossibility of these grimoire instructions.</summary>

<keywords>Éliphas Lévi, ring of Gyges, invisibility, Little Albert, Peter of Apono, hyena hair, magical rings, lapwing nest, planetary magic, Hand of Glory</keywords>

--- Page 249 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the discussion of invisibility rings to the "Hand of Glory," a notorious magical artifact. It introduces the two types of rituals associated with it: one for protection (specifically for burglars) and another for financial gain.</meta>

No person who is familiar with the humor of the *Ingoldsby Legends* <note>A popular 19th-century collection of myths and ghost stories written by Richard Harris Barham</note> will have forgotten the wondrous legend of the Hand of Glory, which includes a transcript from the grimoires. It is less generally known that there are two processes, serving distinct uses, and there can be little doubt that they should both be in the possession of every well-equipped operator who is anxious to test the virtues of White and Black Magic in this particular form. The possession of the first alone might be a source of temptation, as it is designed primarily for the protection of burglars. The second renders housebreaking unnecessary, as

it ensures a decent income <note>original: "competence"</note>, but is at the same time without prejudice to its companion, which might be serviceable in a number of emergencies that are not, at least technically, illegal.

<image-desc>A woodcut-style illustration of a "Hand of Glory." It depicts a severed human hand standing upright on its wrist. The fingers are curled inward, and a lit candle with a single flame rises from the center of the hand.</image-desc>

According to the *Little Albert of Lucius* <note>original: "Albertus Parvus Lucii Libellus," a famous 18th-century French grimoire of natural and cabalistic magic</note>, the Hand of Glory is indifferently the right or left hand of a criminal who has been hanged <note>original: "gibbeted"</note>. The sorcerer obtains it as he can, and in the days of Tyburn Tree <note>The principal site for public executions in London for centuries</note> such requisites might have cost nothing beyond the personal risk of the adventure; it is indispensable, however, that it should be wrapped in a piece of winding-sheet <note>A funeral shroud</note>, and this suggests that the criminal must have been previously cut down with a view to burial. Thus enclosed, the hand must be well squeezed so as to force out any blood which may possibly remain in the limb, after which it must be placed in an earthen vessel, together with some zimort <note>An obscure term probably referring to a type of chemical salt or mineral used in alchemy</note>, saltpeter, common salt, and peppercorns—all pounded together. It should remain in this vessel for fifteen days and when extracted should be exposed to the heat of the sun during the time of the dog-star <note>The "dog days" of summer, associated with the rising of the star Sirius</note> until it is extremely dried out. If solar warmth be insufficient, it may be placed in a furnace, heated with bracken and vervain. The object is to extract all the grease from the limb, and therefrom, in combination

with virgin wax and sesame from Lapland, to compose a species of candle. Wheresoever this frightful object is lighted, the spectators will be deprived of all motion and the <note>The text continues on the next page to describe the paralysis of those who see the candle.</note>

<summary>This page describes the "Hand of Glory," a gruesome magical charm made from the severed hand of a hanged criminal, used to paralyze bystanders when fashioned into a candle.</summary>
<keywords>Hand of Glory, Little Albert, Albertus Parvus, Tyburn Tree, necromancy, burglars' magic, alchemy, Ingoldsby Legends</keywords>

--- Page 250 ---
<meta>This page concludes the discussion of the "Hand of Glory" by describing how to protect a home against its influence and introduces a bizarre alternative ritual involving a magical serpent that acts as a supernatural money-multiplier. It then transitions into techniques for seeing spirits.</meta>

A sorcerer can do whatever he wills. However, it is possible to destroy this influence by anointing the threshold of the door, or other places where someone might enter a house, with an ointment composed of the gall of a black cat, grease from a white fowl, and the blood of a screech-owl. This mixture should also be prepared during the "dog-days" <note>The hottest weeks of summer, typically July and August, traditionally associated with the rising of the star Sirius, the Dog Star</note>.

It is regrettable that this remarkable process does not rest upon the personal testimony of its historian. However, he was present, as reports tell us, at the trials of several people who confessed under torture that they had used it with complete success. It is also regrettable that the alternative experiment must remain, regarding its materials, in the language of its inventor. It will be seen, however, that this version of the "Hand of Glory" is not actually a hand, but a serpent; the process has, in fact, no connection whatever with its name, but this is entirely in accordance with the nature of Black Magic, its variations, and its substitutes.

"Pull a hair out by its root from a mare in heat, as close to its genitals as possible" <note>original: *Arrachez le poil avec sa racine d'une jument en chaleur, le plus près de la nature*</note>, while saying: DRAGNE, DRAGNE, DRAGNE. Tie the hair in a knot and go immediately to purchase a new earthenware pot with a lid, without haggling over the price. Return home at full speed; fill the pot with spring water to within two inches of the rim. Place your material inside, cover the vessel, and set it where neither you nor anyone else can see it, for the Grimoire <note>The *Grimorium Verum* or "True Grimoire," a 16th-century textbook of black magic</note> warns that there is danger in looking. At the end of nine days, and at the hour of concealment <note>Likely referring to dusk or nightfall</note>, bring it out and open it. You will discover a small animal in the form of a serpent, which will immediately spring up. Then say: "I accept the pact." Do not touch the creature with your hand, but transfer it into a new box, purchased specifically for this purpose without bargaining. To feed the monster, give it only wheat husks daily. 

When you are in need of gold or silver, place as much as you need in the box; go to sleep with the box at your bedside for three or four hours. When you rise, you will find double the amount of money you entrusted to the serpent. However, what you originally placed in the box must be left there; it becomes a sort of supernatural savings bank that doubles its capital daily. Should the reptile have an ordinary appearance, you must not try for more than one hundred francs at a time; but if your planet gives you a favorable alignment in supernatural matters, the serpent will have a human face, and you may deposit a thousand francs. In either case, if the operator wishes to get rid of this possession, two conditions must be met: the recipient must agree to accept the gift, and a figure (which must be drawn on virgin parchment, though it was omitted by the Grimoire) must be placed in the box. If, however, you prefer to destroy it, feed the serpent not its usual bran, but some of the flour used for consecration in a priest's first Mass; after eating this, it will die. On the whole, it is simpler to keep the enchanted animal, especially since the vague responsibilities of the magical pact are apparently neither transferred in the first case nor cancelled in the second. No joke is intended, says the dry author of the *True Grimoire*, but the process of the "Gold-Finding Hen" is generally simpler and also cleaner.

### § 5. Concerning the Vision of Spirits in the Air

<summary>This page describes a protective ointment against the Hand of Glory and details a ritual from the Grimorium Verum to create a money-doubling serpent from a mare's hair. It concludes by noting the risks of magical pacts before moving on to techniques for seeing spirits.</summary>

<keywords>Hand of Glory, counter-charms, dog-days, Grimorium Verum, magical pacts, wealth rituals, money-doubling serpent, Gold-Finding Hen, spirit vision</keywords>

--- Page 251 ---
<meta>This page continues the description of rituals for perceiving spirits, moving from a bizarre incense-making process to the specific practice of divining through the spirit Uriel.</meta>

For the Masters of Black Magic, as for the author of the *Count of Gabalis* <note>original: *Comte de Gabalis*, a 17th-century French work by Henri de Montfaucon de Villars discussing elemental spirits</note>, the air is inhabited by beings other than birds and flies. However, the process for making them visible is complicated because of the rare materials required. It is, of course, quite possible to get the brain of a rooster, and the dissection needed to get it can perhaps be done by someone else; a kitchen maid or a butcher’s assistant could easily be hired for the task. The second ingredient is dust from a man’s grave. Simply visiting the nearest cemetery isn't enough, though, because surface dust is useless; only the dust found directly next to the coffin will work. In addition to these, you only need oil of almonds and virgin wax. A mixture must be made of these four items and wrapped in a sheet of virgin parchment <note>parchment made from the skin of a newborn or young animal</note> previously inscribed with the words GOMERT, KAILOETH, and the symbol of *Khil*.

Once the materials are prepared, they must be set on fire. At that point, the practitioner will see what the Grimoire describes as "prodigious," though it does not explain further except for what is suggested in the title. This experiment, it adds, should only be performed by those who fear nothing.

It is easy to mock this process, but thoughtful people will see that it is the very essence and summary of the entire craft. This is Black Magic—and most of the "white" kind—in a nutshell: a combination of equal parts the disgusting and the foolish. There are many more complex experiments, but few are so representative of the genre. It is worth noting that this ritual has been extremely popular and appears in most Grimoires <note>textbooks of magic</note>.

### § 6. Concerning Divination by the Word of Uriel

Just as many practices labeled "White Magic" are questionable on the surface and even more so underneath, there are also experiences described in the Grimoires—or the "treasuries of secrets" that accompany them—that contain nothing particularly repulsive. However, they belong in the Grimoires because their roots in occult literature are tied to Black Magic, so they deserve a place here. Furthermore, a ritual that seems merely curious, entertaining, or related to hypnosis or clairvoyance in one book may have all the outward signs of devil-worship in another. The method of divination called the "Word" or "Speech" of Uriel—referring to answers or oracles received from that spirit through ritual invocation—appears in *The True Grimoire* <note>original: *Grimorium Verum*</note> as an experiment in achieving a trance state through Hydromancy <note>divination using water</note>. However, in *The True Little Book of the Jesuits* <note>original: *Verus Jesuitarum Libellus*</note>, the "Conjuration of Uriel" appears as an appeal to demons and is an undisguised ritual of Black Magic.

<summary>This page describes a ritual for making spirits visible using grave dust and a rooster's brain, then introduces the divination ritual of the "Word of Uriel," which varies between simple clairvoyance and dark conjuration depending on the source.</summary>
<keywords>Black Magic, Count of Gabalis, grave dust, Khil, Uriel, divination, Grimorium Verum, hydromancy, Verus Jesuitarum Libellus</keywords>

--- Page 252 ---
<meta>This page continues the description of rituals for perceiving spirits, moving from a general discussion of the absurdity of certain ingredients to the specific instructions for a ritual from the True Grimoire.</meta>

The experiment in the *True Grimoire* <note>original: *Grimorium Verum*, a famous 18th-century handbook of black magic</note> is written as follows:

## THE INVOCATION OF URIEL

*To succeed in this operation, it is necessary for whoever performs the experiment to follow all the instructions listed below. Let him choose a small room or private study that has not been visited by "unclean" women for at least nine days. Let this place be thoroughly cleaned and consecrated by sprinkling holy water and burning incense. In the middle of the room, place a table covered with a white cloth. Set the following items on it: a new glass bottle <term>phial</term> filled with spring water, collected just before the ritual; three small candles made of virgin wax <note>wax from a beehive that has never been used for breeding</note> mixed with human fat; a six-inch square sheet of virgin parchment <note>parchment made from the skin of a newborn animal</note>; a raven’s quill pen, prepared for writing; a ceramic inkwell filled with fresh ink; and a small pan containing everything needed to start a fire. There must also be a young boy, nine or ten years old, dressed cleanly and modestly and of good character, who should stand near the table. One of the three candles should be stuck onto a large, new needle six inches behind the bottle,*

*and the other two candles, set up the same way, should stand to the right and left at the same distance. While arranging these items, recite these words:*

> Gabamiah, Adonai <note>Hebrew: "My Lord"</note>, Agla <note>A Hebrew kabbalistic acronym for *Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai*, meaning "You, O Lord, are mighty forever"</note>, O Lord God of Powers, help us!

*Place the virgin parchment on the right and the pen and ink on the left side of the bottle. The windows and door must be closed before starting the ritual. The fire should then be stirred, the candles lit, and the boy instructed to kneel so he can look into the bottle. He must be bareheaded with his hands joined in prayer. The leader of the ritual <term>Master</term> will then command him to stare fixedly into the bottle. The leader should then lean toward the boy’s right ear and, in a moderate but very clear voice, speak the following:*

## CONJURATION

> *Uriel, Seraph <note>a high-ranking type of angel</note>, Josata, Ablati, Agla, Caila: I pray and command you by the four words which God spoke with His own mouth to His servant Moses—Josata, Ablati, Agla, Caila—and by the Nine Heavens where you live, and also by the purity of this child standing before you, to appear visibly in this bottle without delay. Reveal the truth I wish to know without hiding anything. Once this is done, I will dismiss you in peace and kindness, in the name of the most holy Adonai.*

*After this command, ask the child if he sees anything in the bottle. If he says he sees an angel or any other spirit, the ritual leader should say in a friendly voice:*

> *Blessed spirit, you are welcome! I command you once more, in the name of the most holy Adonai, to give me quick enlightenment.*

<summary>This page details a ritual from the *Grimorium Verum* for invoking the angel Uriel using a child medium, a glass bottle of water, and candles made with human fat. The practitioner uses specific prayers and commands to make the spirit appear and reveal hidden truths.</summary>
<keywords>Uriel, Grimorium Verum, ritual magic, scrying, child medium, Adonai, Agla, virgin parchment, conjuration</keywords>

upon, etc. [Here name the subject upon which information is desired.] 
> *And if for reasons unknown to us, you are unwilling to proceed in an audible tone of voice, <gloss>1</gloss> I conjure you in the Most Holy Name of Adonai <note>Hebrew: "My Lord"</note> to write upon the virgin parchment <note>parchment made from the skin of a newborn animal</note> provided here, between now and tomorrow morning, or at least reveal what I desire to me in my sleep tonight.*

If the Spirit answers what is said, he should be heard respectfully. Should he fail to speak after the same request has been made three times, let the candles <term>tapers</term> be extinguished and leave the room. Close the door until the next day, when the practitioner <term>operator</term> may return in the morning. What was requested will be found inside on the virgin parchment, unless it was revealed during the night.

In the *True Booklet of the Jesuits* <note>original: *Verum Jesuitarum Libellus*, a forged magical text falsely attributed to the Jesuit Order</note>, this experiment for inducing clairvoyance <note>the ability to perceive things beyond the natural senses</note> through a scrying <term>seering</term> cup and an elaborate ritual—which has only one monstrous condition—is replaced by a fierce conjuration spanning many pages. This ritual commands the Spirit Uriel by all the words ever spoken by the Creator of Heaven and Earth against the Evil Angels. Under threat of force and torture, Uriel is ordered to appear before the practitioner—described as an unworthy servant of God—wherever the spirit may be, even in the depths of Hell. The spirit is directed to appear visibly and modestly in a human form, and to bring whatever is desired in total peace and patience—without noise <term>tumult</term>, harm, blinding, loss of speech, whispering, thunder, hail, explosions, swelling <term>puffing up</term>, or trembling. It is unnecessary to quote the entire ritual, as the

<meta>p. 318</meta>

process is incomplete; but it is clear that the "Conjuration of Uriel" in the forged treatise on the Magic of the Jesuits is intended to force a demon to obey, rather than to ask for favors from the Blessed Spirit mentioned in the *True Grimoire* <note>original: *Grimorium Verum*</note>.

## § 7. Concerning the Mirror of Solomon, suitable for all Kinds of Divination

The following process is found in the *True Grimoire* <note>original: *Grimorium Verum*</note> and some other collections. It is a method of divination <note>seeking knowledge of the unknown by supernatural means</note> similar to the one in the previous section. It is not related to Black Magic except for the use of blood; in reality, it is an experiment in self-hypnosis <term>auto-hypnotic</term> strengthened by traditional ceremonies.

->How to Create the Mirror of Solomon<-

<summary>This page concludes the ritual for invoking the spirit Uriel and compares the version in the "True Grimoire" to a more aggressive, demonic version found in a forged Jesuit text. It also introduces the "Mirror of Solomon," a tool for divination and self-hypnosis.</summary>
<keywords>Uriel, Grimorium Verum, Verum Jesuitarum Libellus, scrying, Mirror of Solomon, divination, John Dee, ceremonial magic, self-hypnosis</keywords>

--- Page 254 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for rituals of divination, moving from the "Invocation of Uriel" to the "Mirror of Solomon," a method for scrying using a polished steel plate and the invocation of the angel Anaël.</meta>

*In the Name of the Lord, Amen. You shall behold in this mirror all things whatsoever you may desire. In the Name of the Lord who is blessed, in the Name of the Lord, Amen.*

In the first place, you shall perform no physical <note>original: "fleshly"</note> actions, nor sin in thought or deed, during the period prescribed below. In the second place, you shall perform many good works of piety and mercy. In the third place, take a shining and well-polished plate of fine steel, slightly concave <note>curved inward like a shallow bowl</note>, and with the blood of a white pigeon inscribe upon it—specifically, at the four corners—the names JEHOVA, ELOYM, METATRON, ADONAY. Place this steel plate in a clean, white cloth. Now, when you see the new moon during the first hour after sunset, go to a window, look up to Heaven with devotion, and say: 

"O Eternal! O King Eternal! God indescribable <note>original: "ineffable"</note>! You who have created all things for the love of men, and by a secret judgment for the spiritual health of man, graciously deign to look upon me, [Name] <note>The letter "N." stands for *Nomen*, indicating where the practitioner should state their own name</note>, Your most unworthy servant, and upon this my intention. Grant <term>vouchsafe</term> to send Your Angel ANAËL unto this mirror, he who orders, commands, and ordains his companions and Your subjects, whom You have made, O Almighty..."

> *Lord, who have been, who are, and who shall remain eternally, so that in Your Name they may judge and act justly, instructing me in all that I shall require of them.*

Lastly, cast a suitable incense <note>original: "perfume"</note> upon burning coals, and while casting it say:

<page-num>320</page-num>

> *In this, by this, and with this, which I pour out before Your face, O God, my God, who are blessed, Three and One, and in the most sublime exaltation, who sit above the Cherubim and above the Seraphim, who will judge the world by fire, hear me!*

Repeat this three times, and having done so, breathe three times upon the mirror and say:

> *Come, ANAËL <note>Anaël is traditionally the angel associated with the planet Venus and human love</note>, come, and may it be your good pleasure to be with me by your will, in the Name of the Father most mighty, in the Name of the Son most wise, in the Name of the Holy Spirit most living! Come, ANAËL, in the Name of the terrible Jehovah <note>a rendering of the Hebrew Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God</note>! Come, ANAËL, by the virtue of the immortal Elohim <note>Hebrew: "God" or "Gods," a common divine title in grimoires</note>! Come, ANAËL, by the right arm of the almighty METATRON <note>in Jewish mysticism, the highest of the angels who serves as the celestial scribe</note>! Come to me, [Name] (here repeat your name over the mirror), and so command your subjects that in love, joy, and peace, they may make manifest to my eyes the things which are hidden from me. So be it. Amen.*

Having said and done as described above, lift your eyes to heaven and say:

> *O Lord Almighty, who causes all things to move according to Your good pleasure, hear my prayer, and may my desire be agreeable to You! Lord, O Lord, if You will, condescend to look upon this mirror and bless it, so that ANAËL, one of Your servants, may pause there with his companions to satisfy me, [Name], Your poor and humble servant, O God, blessed and exalted above all the heavenly Spirits, who live and reign for ever and ever. Amen.*

When you have accomplished these things, make the sign of the Cross upon yourself and upon the mirror every day for forty-five days in succession. At the end of this time, Anaël will appear to you in the form of a beautiful child, will salute you, and will command his companions to obey you. Observe—as the ritual says in its

<page-num>321</page-num>

kindness <note>original: "benignity"</note>—that it does not always require forty-five days to prepare the mirror. The Angel will frequently appear on the fourteenth day, depending on the intention, devotion, and fervor of the practitioner <note>original: "operator"</note>. When he appears, ask of him what you desire, and pray for him to come at all times, whenever you shall call upon <term>invoke</term> him, to grant your requests. On later occasions, when you wish to see in this mirror, it is not necessary to recite all the prayers provided above; instead, after having perfumed it with incense, say only:

> *Come ANAËL, come, according to your good pleasure, etc.*

To dismiss him, say:

> *I thank you, ANAËL, because you have appeared and have satisfied my demands. Therefore, depart in peace, and return when I shall call you.*

The incense <note>original: "perfume"</note> of Anaël is saffron.

<meta>This page concludes the ritual for the "Mirror of Solomon" and begins a new section regarding the "Three Rings of Solomon." # § 8. Concerning the Three Rings of Solomon, Son of David

One more procedure <note>original: "experiment"</note>, which is only indirectly <note>original: "incidentally"</note> connected with Black Magic, may be cited here as a supplement to those given in the preceding sections. It is hardly a complete ritual and is not fully understandable as it stands in the *Grimorium Verum* <note>Latin: "The True Grimoire," a 18th-century handbook of magic attributed to a 16th-century source</note>, where the symbols <note>original: "characters"</note> on which it depends are missing. The system assumes that the practitioner is in possession of a talisman inscribed with the Pentacles of Solomon <note>Pentacles are magical diagrams or amulets used in evocation to protect the magician and command spirits</note>, at which point he may proceed according to the following directions:

Prepare <note>original: "Compose"</note> your circle, but before entering it, scent it with musk, amber, aloeswood, and incense. When performing the invocation, the scent should be incense only. Take care also that

<margin>Page 322</margin>

you have a fire whenever you make an invocation, and burn incense <note>original: "fumigate"</note> only in the name of the Spirit whom you intend to call. When placing the incense on the fire, say: *I burn this [Name of incense] in the name and to the honor of [Name of Spirit].* When invoking, ensure that you hold the written invocation in your left hand, while holding the rod made of elder wood in your right. The ladle and knife should be placed at your feet. Once these things are arranged, place yourself within the circle; if you are accompanied, your companions should hold each other by one hand. When inside, trace the circle with the *Knife of the Art* <note>a consecrated ritual knife used in ceremonial magic</note>, and take up the rods one after the other, reciting the Fiftieth Psalm, namely, *Have mercy on me* <note>original: "Miserere mei," the opening words of Psalm 51 in the Vulgate Bible</note>. Once the circle is prepared, scent it and sprinkle it with holy water. Then write the symbols at the four corners; the Spirit must be forbidden in formal terms from entering the circle, after which you begin the invocations, which must be repeated seven times in a row. When the Spirit has appeared, make him sign the symbol which you hold in your hands, promising to come whenever you call. Ask for whatever you think is appropriate, and you shall be satisfied. Dismiss him with these words: *Go in peace to your own places, and may peace be with you until you return at my call. In the name of [the Father, etc.]. Amen.*
